     1	Cancer dependency research with an autonomous RSI loop
     2	
     3	Goal and executable handoff for a local research agent
     4	
     5	Prepared 28 October 2026. Treat every source below as a seed to verify against the current primary source, release manifest, and paper methods.
     6	
     7	
     8	1 - The ambitious goal
     9	
    10	Build and run a laptop-based autonomous research system that answers a specific, still-open question about when molecularly defined cancer dependencies persist or fail across measured culture environments.
    11	
    12	The primary question is whether measured sensitivity to culture conditions, together with genotype and tumor state, can explain failures of dependency transfer from conventional cancer models to patient-derived organoids beyond lineage and technical variation alone.
    13	
    14	Produce all of the following.
    15	
    16	- At least one previously unreported, precisely bounded, externally supported explanatory finding. It may be a positive conditional dependency rule, a convincing refutation of a meaningful dependency claim, or an informative bound that materially changes how a target should be interpreted. It must resolve a specific uncertainty, not merely increase a benchmark score.
    17	- A reproducible evidence atlas covering every eligible gene in the controlled environmental panel, including support, contradiction, heterogeneity, uncertainty, missing evidence, and excluded genes with reasons. Expansion beyond that selected panel is exploratory unless independently justified.
    18	- A defensible connection between measured environmental sensitivity and transfer behavior in an independently filtered organoid cohort, where the available evidence supports that connection.
    19	- An implemented research-policy improvement loop with real experiments, faithful historical replay, executable policy revisions, fresh deployment, and a final comparison against strong fixed exploration strategies.
    20	- Evidence that the learned policy produces a practically meaningful improvement under matched total resources, including its own learning overhead.
    21	- A manuscript and complete reproducibility package ready for independent expert review, with all findings, failures, limitations, and costs disclosed.
    22	
    23	Seek a finding that changes which cancer target evidence researchers should trust. Do not substitute a generic prediction leaderboard or an agent framework demonstration for that scientific objective.
    24	
    25	Ambition does not authorize changing the standard of evidence. If the scientific result or the policy improvement fails its criteria, the overall goal remains unmet. A valid negative result can qualify only when it resolves a predeclared meaningful uncertainty with sufficient precision. An insignificant p-value does not qualify.
    26

    28	2 - Your operating role
    29	
    30	You are the lead investigator, research engineer, statistician, and experiment coordinator for this project.
    31	
    32	Execute the work. Do not stop after writing a plan, creating folders, or describing an architecture. Use the current session to establish the scientific contract, build the smallest working vertical slice, evaluate real public observations, and begin the first actual policy-improvement cycle when the evidence and resources permit.
    33	
    34	Use frontier model reasoning through the capabilities already supplied by your host agent. All scientific fitting, simulation, evaluation, and data processing must run on this laptop. Public web access and public data services are allowed. No new wet-lab measurements, controlled-access patient data, paid scientific cloud compute, or local foundation-model training are required.
    35	
    36	Make routine research and implementation decisions autonomously. Ask only when a missing credential, explicit spending authority, unavailable required file, or genuinely consequential unresolved user choice prevents further authorized work. Complete all independent work first.
    37

    84	
    85	The starting literature establishes several constraints.
    86	
    87	- The Broad resource reports 147 genome-scale screens, while the Sanger resource reports screens across 162 organoids. These counts are not automatically disjoint biological samples.
    88	- The smaller controlled Broad environmental experiment has 250 deliberately selected test genes and 18 screens across only seven biological models. It includes seven matched format contrasts and five matched medium contrasts, with possible repeated models. Verify the exact design in the source.
    89	- The 250 genes were selected partly because they differed between model environments. They are not a representative genome-wide sample.
    90	- The October 2026 transferability study already examines expression-based transfer from conventional models to Broad and Sanger organoids for 20 selected genes. Generic 2D-to-organoid transfer is not the new contribution.
    91	- Broad's temporary NextGen manuscript dataset was incorporated into DepMap Public 26Q3. Staff direct users to the integrated CRISPRGeneEffect.csv and relevant model metadata. Reverify the current route rather than inventing a file ID or assuming old instructions still work.
    92	- Sanger processed fitness measurements and Broad Chronos gene effects are different measurement systems. Their absolute values are not interchangeable.
    93	- Historical releases, a current integrated release, and independently produced laboratory data are different forms of evidence. Reprocessing the same observations is not biological replication.
    94	
    95	Resolve downloads through current official manifests. Record source URL, release or commit, retrieval date, license, byte size, and checksum. Do not invent direct download URLs or accession metadata. Use official alternative routes if a public portal blocks unattended retrieval. Do not bypass a challenge or access control.
    96	
    97	Build around public processed observations. Raw sequencing is unnecessary. If a proposed analysis requires paired observations that are not actually released, identify that limitation before implementing the model.

   100	4 - Hardware, budget, and persistence
   101	
   102	Inspect the available RAM, CPU, disk, operating system, installed runtimes, and already configured model access without printing secrets.
   103	
   104	Set conservative defaults and save them in a machine-readable configuration.
   105	
   106	- No GPU dependency.
   107	- At most two CPU-heavy jobs concurrently.
   108	- An aggregate research memory ceiling of the smaller of 8 GiB and 60 percent of physical RAM, reduced further when necessary to leave the host responsive.
   109	- A ten-minute default timeout for an individual exploratory fitting job. Longer jobs require an explicit recorded allocation from the existing campaign budget.
   110	- A download ceiling of 20 GiB or one quarter of available disk space, whichever is smaller.
   111	- A first pilot target of 24 real evaluated development nodes and three executable policy challengers, retaining the incumbent.
   112	- An eight-hour ceiling for the first pilot invocation. This is a safety ceiling, not a promised completion time.
   113	- No automatic increase in financial, disk, concurrency, or wall-time ceilings.
   114	
   115	Use the host agent's already authorized model access. Any additional programmatic metered API calls require a numeric user-configured cap or an existing explicitly authorized cap. If none exists, the additional API spending cap is zero. Continue with host-driven reasoning and local computation where possible, and build the adapter without dispatching unapproved calls.
   116	
   117	Do not require the user to buy new services just to start the project. Do not silently equate an unknown price with zero.
   118	
   119	For separately dispatched API calls, enforce limits in code. Reserve a conservative maximum cost before each request, count concurrent reservations and retries, reconcile against returned usage, and persist the ledger before dispatch. Do not call a provider whose billable maximum cannot be bounded by the configured limits.
   120	
   121	Record model identifiers, prompts, token counts when exposed, tool costs, wall time, CPU time, and peak memory. If host-model costs cannot be measured, mark them unknown. You may still do science, but you cannot certify an all-in cost improvement while material costs are unobserved.
   122	
   123	Use append-only event records and checkpoint after every completed experiment. Track proposed, dispatched, completed, failed, and committed states. An interrupted request remains reserved until reconciled. Never reset the budget or repeat a completed paid request on resume.
   124

   128	5 - Data and identifiability audit
   129	
   130	Before adaptive hypothesis search, construct a provenance table with biological model, donor, tumor lineage, sample origin, laboratory, assay library, medium, growth format, replicate identity, release, and measured outcome.
   131	
   132	Create explicit crosswalks among Broad, HCMI, Sanger, and author-specific identifiers. Mark uncertain donor identity as uncertain rather than assuming independence.
   133	
   134	Determine which observations are truly paired. Count independent biological models and donors. Do not count genes, guide RNAs, wells, alternative cultures, or technical replicates as additional independent patients.
   135	
   136	Audit release composition and preprocessing provenance. An integrated release may contain validation observations or corrections learned jointly from them. Removing overlapping rows alone does not undo leakage through outcome-guided target selection, normalization, or model selection. Prefer compatible study-specific releases and transformations fitted on development data. If a fitted correction used external dependency outcomes, rebuild a development-only analysis or obtain genuinely independent validation evidence. Disclosure alone does not restore independence. Distinguish that outcome leakage from covariate-only joint preprocessing, which must be disclosed as transductive feature preprocessing with appropriately limited claims. This is a required provenance check, not an assertion that any named release is contaminated.
   137	
   138	Verify dependency score direction, dynamic range, controls, missingness, and quality flags separately for each source. Avoid direct subtraction of Chronos values from Sanger fold-change or essentiality scores.
   139	
   140	Preserve the seven-model environmental design as a small, selected experiment. Determine whether row-level paired outcomes, replicates, and required metadata are sufficient for the intended contrast. A batch correction cannot separate laboratory and biology when the experimental design confounds them.
   141	
   142	Record attrition explicitly, including models unable to grow in a condition and genes excluded for insufficient variation or precision.
   143	
   144	Data eligibility is determined from prespecified quality and identifiability rules. It must not be selected later to maximize a favorable result.
   145	
   146	The gate is an identifiable, falsifiable question in a named subset. If the data cannot support the original question, propose and document a narrower still-open question within the same scientific objective. Do not pretend the original broader goal has been achieved.
   147	
   148	
   149	6 - Scientific estimands and research contract
   150	
   151	Start from two connected estimands.
   152	
   153	The direct environmental estimand is the change in a gene's measured dependency between environment A and environment B within the same biological model, while holding the other measured culture condition fixed. Analyze medium and growth format separately where the design permits.
   154	
   155	The transfer estimand is the extent to which a prespecified genotype, tumor-state, or context relationship predicts dependency behavior in an independently filtered organoid cohort beyond lineage and the strongest appropriate baseline.
   156	
   157	Use original documented assay units for paired within-study contrasts. Use justified ranking, calibration, or outcome-specific comparisons for cross-study evidence.
   158	
   159	For each environmental claim, report the dependency in both conditions, all individual paired changes, uncertainty, and heterogeneity. A near-zero average can conceal large opposing effects.
   160	
   161	Do not call a dependency invariant because a difference is nonsignificant. If claiming equivalence, define a scientifically justified equivalence margin before confirmatory evaluation and require the relevant interval to fall inside that margin. If no defensible margin exists, report continuous effects and uncertainty without an invariance label.
   162	
   163	A stable absence of dependency is not a stable cancer vulnerability. Establish that the dependency itself is meaningful in the environments covered by a positive claim.
   164	
   165	Do not interpret a predictive expression feature as a causal mediator without identifying assumptions and suitable evidence. Environmental intervention evidence and cross-cohort biomarker associations must retain distinct evidence labels.
   166	
   167	Carry uncertainty and shared-model dependence in estimated environmental sensitivity into downstream transfer analyses using a justified measurement-error, resampling, or sensitivity analysis. Do not treat noisy estimated sensitivities as exact independent inputs. Report conclusions that change materially across plausible environmental-effect estimates.
   168	
   169	During initial development, choose and justify the precise primary endpoint, practical effect margin, hypothesis family, baseline set, uncertainty procedure, and sensitivity analyses. Then freeze them before policy optimization.
   170	
   171	The research contract must include the following.
   172	
   173	- Exact question and falsifiable alternatives.
   174	- Intended population, environments, targets, and generalization claim.
   175	- Eligibility and exclusion rules.
   176	- Data and identifier versions.
   177	- Development, policy-evaluation, and final external partitions.
   178	- Feature engineering and transformation rules.
   179	- Primary and secondary endpoints with fixed direction.
   180	- Minimum practical effects or equivalence margins and their justification.
   181	- Multiplicity control and uncertainty methods.
   182	- Baseline definitions.
   183	- Invalid-result rules.
   184	- Success, failure, inconclusive, and budget-exhaustion conditions.
   185	
   186	The initial lock fixes the permitted hypothesis and transformation spaces, partitions, evaluator, baseline definitions, practical margins, and inferential rules. Adaptive development may select hypotheses, features, hyperparameters, and fitted artifacts within those spaces. A second lock commits the exact selected claim family, predictions, and artifacts before external evaluation.
   187	
   188	Hash this contract, evaluator code, and split manifests. Changing a locked scientific contract creates a new study identifier and requires appropriate new evaluation evidence.
   189	
   190	
   191	7 - Novelty and biological scope
   192	
   193	Maintain a claim ledger throughout the project.
   194	
   195	For each proposed claim, record the nearest prior result, which part is already established, the precise remaining uncertainty, evidence needed to resolve it, and the consequence if the claim is supported or refuted.
   196	
   197	Read the Broad environmental analyses, the Sanger biomarker analyses, and the October transfer study closely. Their reported lipid, adhesion, state, and dependency findings are controls or prior evidence. Recovering them is not a new discovery.
   198	
   199	Use current primary literature, author data, and official documentation. Date the search and preserve queries and important exclusions. Absence of an exact search hit is not proof that a claim is new.
   200	
   201	Choose a bounded hypothesis family before final evaluation. Consider gene classes with measured environmental sensitivity and adequate overlap, not an unconstrained scan of every feature until a small p-value appears.
   202	
   203	Before finalizing the manuscript, repeat the novelty audit for each surviving exact claim. Preserve contradictions and prior evidence. Do not silently rename a known association to make it appear new.
   204	
   205	The required scientific contribution must be more specific than weak organoid transfer, better average correlation, or successful agent automation.
   206	
   207	
   208	8 - Baselines, inference, and adversarial checks
   209	
   210	Build simple competent baselines before allowing elaborate models.
   211	
   212	Before the final lock, reproduce baselines only on development data or independent control data. Any reproduction requiring final external outcomes must occur after the final hypotheses, predictions, artifacts, and inferential code are committed, alongside the final evaluation. Those results cannot inform revisions to the tested study.
   213	
   214	Where applicable compare lineage only, genotype only, tumor-state or expression only, genotype plus state, measured context or assay covariates, and a regularized linear predictor modeled on the existing transfer study.
   215	
   216	Use the same eligible observations and permitted information for each comparison. Learn preprocessing, feature selection, dimension reduction, and tuning within development folds. Do not optimize a feature representation on final external outcomes.
   217	
   218	Compare against the strongest applicable baseline, not only the weakest. If environmental information is central to the explanation, include a prespecified ablation removing it.
   219	
   220	Use effect sizes and calibrated predictive or uncertainty endpoints as the scientific evaluator's optimization target. Do not reward p-value reduction, the number of discoveries, manuscript length, or confidence of the LLM explanation.
   221	
   222	Treat adaptive development as exploratory. Evaluate a locked final claim family once on independent outcomes. For a small confirmatory family, Holm correction at familywise alpha 0.05 is a reasonable default. Any alternative must be justified and frozen before outcomes are revealed.
   223	
   224	Do not apply ordinary fixed-family correction to an indefinitely adaptive stream of repeatedly inspected p-values and call it confirmatory evidence.
   225	
   226	Use uncertainty at the donor or biological-model level, accounting for repeated cultures and target or pathway dependence where relevant. Many bootstrap repetitions do not create new biological replication. With few clusters, assess whether the proposed interval or test is justified and report the effective sample size.
   227	
   228	Perform relevant negative controls, leakage checks, donor and lineage sensitivity analyses, platform sensitivity, and leave-one-model-out checks. Permutations must respect exchangeability and grouping. Do not permute labels freely when the design makes that invalid.
   229	
   230	Synthetic data may test code, calibration, and identifiability under explicit assumptions. Synthetic labels, imputed outcomes, and LLM guesses cannot serve as independent biological validation.
   231	
   232	Only make claims supported by the actual evidence. Public dependency screens alone do not establish clinical efficacy, normal-tissue safety, treatment resistance reversal, or universal target validity.
   233	
   234	
   235	9 - Three distinct evaluation layers
   236	
   237	Keep these layers separate.
   238	
   239	A - Development task panels
   240	
   241	Use them to build scientific artifacts, tune development models, generate discovery trees, and select policy challengers. Record every adaptive use.
   242	
   243	B - Reserved policy evaluation panels
   244	
   245	Use them to compare the final learned exploration policy with fixed strategies in fresh rollouts. Freeze the compared policies and selection criterion before these panels are evaluated.
   246	
   247	C - Final external biological evaluation
   248	
   249	Prefer an independently filtered Sanger cohort after resolving donor overlap, release provenance, assay compatibility, and adequate representation. Use final outcomes only after committing the hypotheses, predictions, model artifacts, and inferential code.
   250	
   251	A final external cohort cannot also be a repeatedly consulted policy reward. Do not send final Sanger outcomes into the replay pool.
   252	
   253	For each final claim, identify exactly which component each dataset validates. External confirmation of dependency transfer does not independently validate a medium or format effect, or establish that environmental sensitivity caused transfer failure. Retain adaptively reused environmental evidence as exploratory or selection-adjusted evidence, as justified. Do not manufacture an independent environmental test by splitting genes from the same seven models. Seek an additional existing public controlled-environment dataset when the selected claim requires independent environmental confirmation. If none is suitable, narrow the claim or retain the unmet evidence gate.
   254	
   255	Subdividing the selected 250 genes does not create hundreds of independent biological tasks. Record model, donor, gene, and pathway overlap in all panels, and use blocked evaluation. The larger development cohorts may provide engineering tasks for policy learning, but their transfer to the small environmental problem must be reported honestly.
   256	
   257	Within a single privileged local agent, enforce a logged protocol with a separate evaluator interface and restricted inputs. Do not claim cryptographic secrecy or genuine blindness merely because an outcome file lives in a separate directory.
   258	
   259	Use retrospective external validation as the default wording. Public literature and model pretraining can expose outcomes. Record known exposure and do not certify that public results were unknowable.
   260	
   261	A failed final test is a failed final test. Subsequent work that uses its outcomes becomes exploratory unless genuinely new independent evidence is obtained.
   262	
   263	
   264	10 - Implement the actual RSI loop
   265	
   266	Keep the base scientific generator, its model version and prompting contract, data contract, and evaluator fixed during a policy comparison.
   267	
   268	Make the editable policy an executable module that receives observed history, legal actions, and remaining resources, and selects an action.
   269	
   270	Legal actions may include opening a predefined analysis family, choosing an existing branch, refining a particular artifact, running a permitted diagnostic, and stopping.
   271	
   272	The policy may alter branching, prioritization, exploration versus refinement, and stopping. It cannot change labels, splits, metrics, practical margins, multiplicity correction, or success criteria.
   273	
   274	A narrative reflection is not a policy improvement unless it changes executed decisions.
   275	
   276	Record each discovery node's immutable parent, exact artifact, input context hash, data and split hashes, evaluator hash, seeds, result, validity diagnostics, failures, model calls, and measured costs.
   277	
   278	Historical replay may reveal only previously executed artifacts and their actual recorded outcomes. It must not use a model to invent outcomes for unexplored analyses.
   279	
   280	A reordered branch may alter the context seen by a generator. Make refinement depend on an immutable parent and fixed context, or require an exact context match before replaying a recorded child. Do not assume a recorded child represents an arbitrary counterfactual generation.
   281	
   282	The replay policy sees only the outcomes revealed along its trajectory. Do not expose the full tree's future rewards.
   283	
   284	Mark unsupported replay actions unsupported. Do not assign them fake scores or treat a surrogate prediction as a measured outcome. Compare policies on common support, report support coverage, and use replay as a screening tool.
   285	
   286	Perform the following cycle.
   287	
   288	1. Run the initial fixed policy on real development tasks.
   289	2. Preserve the evaluated discovery trees as replay worlds.
   290	3. Generate at least three executable policy challengers plus the unchanged incumbent.
   291	4. Replay them under the same resource constraints and support rules.
   292	5. Select a challenger with the frozen selection rule.
   293	6. Test the challenger in fresh development rollouts against fixed exploration using the same generator and evaluator.
   294	7. Promote only when the preregistered criterion is met.
   295	8. Retain the incumbent when challengers fail.
   296	9. Add only development histories and repeat.
   297	
   298	Complete at least two genuine cycles before the final policy comparison if the authorized resources and valid tasks permit. Two cycles do not require two promotions. An unchanged incumbent is a valid experimental outcome.
   299	
   300	After development, freeze the best admissible policy and evaluate it on the reserved policy panels. Compare it with the original fixed exploration strategy and at least one simple fixed-allocation or random-selection strategy.
   301	
   302	
   303	11 - What counts as demonstrated policy improvement
   304	
   305	Predeclare one primary operating point or cost-to-quality endpoint. Also report complete scientific-quality-versus-cost curves.
   306	
   307	Use the same generator, evaluator, initial artifacts, information permissions, and common resource ceilings for all policies.
   308	
   309	Charge policy rewriting, replay, failed jobs, promotion trials, and extra model calls to the improving system. Permit fixed baselines to spend the corresponding budget on scientific search.
   310	
   311	Report both end-to-end performance including learning overhead and deployment-only performance with learning costs shown separately.
   312	
   313	The ambitious engineering target is at least 20 percent lower total measured API expenditure to reach a predeclared valid scientific-quality threshold, under the same CPU ceiling and information constraints, compared with the strongest fixed baseline. This is a project target, not a forecast.
   314	
   315	Set the quality threshold from development evidence before final policy evaluation. It must be meaningful and attainable by competent baselines, not chosen after observing which policy wins.
   316	
   317	Use paired blocked comparisons where appropriate and report uncertainty. For the default 20 percent savings goal, require the prespecified one-sided 95 percent lower confidence bound on savings to meet the 20 percent practical margin. A point estimate above 20 percent with a lower bound merely above zero supports some improvement but does not meet that ambitious margin. Freeze how failures to reach the quality threshold within budget are treated as censored outcomes. Do not convert nonattainment into invented exact costs. Random seeds quantify execution variability, not independent biological replication.
   318	
   319	If API expenditure is not the dominant or measurable resource, choose a justified alternative primary resource endpoint during the initial contract stage, explain it, and lock it before optimization. All other material resources must still be measured or strictly matched. A CPU-only advantage or token proxy cannot satisfy C9 while material host-model costs remain unknown and unmatched. In that case retain an unresolved cost gate and avoid claiming all-in savings. Define an equally explicit practical improvement margin and uncertainty requirement for any alternative endpoint. Do not switch endpoints after seeing final results.
   320	
   321	If faithful replay has inadequate support, preserve that finding and use fresh rollouts to assess the proposed policy. Label the method accurately as a Dream-RSI-inspired adaptation and disclose departures.
   322	
   323	Do not call the system recursively self-improving merely because more compute improved a reused validation score.
   324	
   325	
   326	12 - Completion criteria
   327	
   328	Mark the ambitious goal complete only when every applicable criterion below has documentary evidence.
   329	
   330	C1 - A current novelty audit identifies a precise unresolved cancer dependency question and distinguishes it from the August resources and October transfer study.
   331	
   332	C2 - All analyses use legitimately accessible public data, with complete provenance, licensing, identifier crosswalks, and reproducible retrieval or documented local cache installation.
   333	
   334	C3 - The intended paired contrast or conditional relationship is identifiable within a named population. Confounding and selection are disclosed, and independent biological units are counted correctly.
   335	
   336	C4 - The scientific contract, eligibility rules, splits, baseline set, practical effects, evaluator, and confirmatory procedures were frozen before adaptive optimization and final evaluation.
   337	
   338	C5 - The pipeline reproduces relevant published or author-code baseline results closely enough to establish outcome direction, cohort integrity, and measurement interpretation, with discrepancies resolved or explicitly bounded.
   339	
   340	C6 - At least one previously unreported explanatory finding passes the frozen scientific criteria and relevant external tests. A supported refutation or informative bound may qualify when it resolves a meaningful predeclared uncertainty. Mere nonsignificance, a rediscovered association, or a better exploratory score does not qualify.
   341	
   342	C7 - The evidence atlas covers all eligible genes in the controlled panel and lists every exclusion. Every entry distinguishes dependency strength, environmental change, uncertainty, heterogeneity, biomarker evidence, transfer evidence, and prior knowledge.
   343	
   344	C8 - At least two real policy-improvement cycles have run, with executable challengers, faithful replay, fresh rollout evaluation, retained failed attempts, and a final frozen-policy comparison on reserved panels.
   345	
   346	C9 - The learned policy meets the preregistered practical improvement criterion against the strongest fixed baseline under matched total resources. Meeting only a deployment-only criterion does not satisfy an end-to-end claim.
   347	
   348	C10 - Scientific conclusions survive the prespecified multiplicity correction, appropriate uncertainty analysis, key ablations, and adversarial checks. Any claim of invariance requires positive dependency evidence and justified equivalence evidence.
   349	
   350	C11 - A clean reproduction run regenerates the primary estimates, figures, evidence decisions, and policy comparison within documented tolerances from frozen artifacts. It must not require rerunning stochastic LLM search to reproduce the reported analysis.
   351	
   352	C12 - The package includes a manuscript for expert review, machine-readable evidence, source and data manifests, all experiment histories, model and policy versions, actual resource measurements, failures, limitations, and exact reproduce and resume commands.
   353	
   354	C13 - The first meaningful end-to-end research cycle has actually executed. A plan, scaffold, mock evaluator, or hypothetical example is insufficient.
   355	
   356	Seek multiple convergent findings across adequately represented lineages and pathways as an additional scientific stretch target. Do not replace one strong finding with a quota of weak associations. Do not force claims about sparsely represented cancer types.
   357	
   358	Keep scientific discovery, prediction improvement, and policy improvement as separate status fields. One does not establish the others.
   359	
   360	
   361	13 - Required artifacts and commands
   362	
   363	Create a compact repository with equivalent artifacts to the following. Choose actual file formats suited to the content.
   364	
   365	- README.md with scope, installation, exact commands, and current limitations.
   366	- GOAL.md with the locked goal and completion criteria.
   367	- PROTOCOL.md and a machine-readable locked research contract.
   368	- STATUS.md and a structured status record with current phase, incumbent, spending, next action, and unmet gates.
   369	- A data manifest, source manifest, donor/model crosswalk, eligibility registry, and split manifests.
   370	- A novelty and claim ledger with the complete decision history.
   371	- Data adapters, baseline models, a fixed evaluator, policy modules, a faithful replay engine, a local runner, and a budget wrapper.
   372	- An append-only experiment ledger and immutable discovery artifacts.
   373	- Frozen final predictions, scientific models, policies, and checksums.
   374	- An evidence atlas in a machine-readable table.
   375	- Figures, a manuscript draft, a limitations statement, and an adversarial-review report.
   376	- Environment and dependency locks, licenses and attribution, and a reproducibility report.
   377	- Meaningful tests for identity leakage, score direction, grouping, estimator behavior, cost accounting, resume idempotence, unsupported replay actions, and final-outcome access.
   378	
   379	Provide simple documented commands equivalent to bootstrap, audit-data, reproduce-baselines, run-pilot, run-cycle, evaluate-policy, lock-science, evaluate-external, reproduce, status, and resume.
   380	
   381	Do not overbuild a general agent platform before the first valid scientific vertical slice works. Reuse maintained components where that reduces risk, but inspect their resource demands and permissions.
   382	
   383	
   384	14 - Terminal outcomes and reporting
   385	
   386	Use explicit terminal states.
   387	
   388	- COMPLETE_SUPPORTED_DISCOVERY
   389	- COMPLETE_SUPPORTED_REFUTATION_OR_BOUND
   390	- SCIENTIFIC_RESULT_WITHOUT_RSI_EVIDENCE
   391	- RSI_RESULT_WITHOUT_SCIENTIFIC_DISCOVERY
   392	- INCONCLUSIVE
   393	- BLOCKED
   394	- BUDGET_EXHAUSTED
   395	
   396	The first two require every relevant completion gate, including demonstrated policy improvement. The others are honest outcomes and must list which parts of the ambitious goal remain unmet.
   397	
   398	A completed computational campaign is not automatically a completed scientific goal. Do not relabel insufficient power as equivalence or a polished manuscript as a discovery.
   399	
   400	When blocked or budget-limited, checkpoint all valid work, identify the exact missing condition, and provide the smallest concrete next step. Do not lower scientific standards, spend beyond the cap, or retry indefinitely.
   401	
   402	During execution, report concise milestones with measured results, unresolved risks, and the next discriminating experiment. Avoid progress theater based on file counts or unexecuted plans.
   403	
   404	
   405	15 - Start now
   406	
   407	Inspect the workspace and resource limits. Verify the primary sources and accessible processed data. Build the donor and assay provenance audit. Formulate a narrowly identifiable first question and its strongest alternative explanations.
   408	
   409	Implement the smallest valid data-to-estimate-to-evaluator path. Reproduce the necessary baseline. Freeze the initial research contract and evaluation partitions.
   410	
   411	Then run real development experiments, build the first replay world, generate executable policy challengers, and perform a fresh comparison. Continue through the authorized staged campaign.
   412	
   413	Your first substantive report must show what was actually executed, what the observations establish, what remains unidentifiable, which policy was retained or promoted, the measured costs, and the exact next action. If a real evidence or authorization gate prevents the first cycle, report that precise blocker and all completed independent work. Do not claim that the loop started when only its scaffolding exists.

cancer-dependency-rsi/reviews/novelty_audit_v1.md
     1	# Novelty audit v1 — 2026-10-28 (Phase 1f; refresh required at G7 + terminal determination)
     2	
     3	## Method
     4	Full-text reads (Europe PMC XML, `data/papers/`): S1 PMC13581591, S2 PMC13581617, S3 PMC13664187. Dated Europe PMC searches 2026-10-28 (queries preserved in session record): culture/CRISPR/dependency/organoid (7 hits), culture-conditions transfer (14 hits), medium/essentiality 2025–2026 (176 hits, top reviewed). **Absence of a search hit is not proof of novelty** — claims below are candidates pending G1 review.
     5	
     6	## Already established (controls / priors — NOT novelty)
     7	1. **S1 (Broad NextGen):** class-level environmental effects in the 250-gene minipool — integrin/actin classes vary by **format** (plastic vs dome), lipid-metabolism class by **medium** (RPMI-FBS vs OPAC), via OLS on 18 screens/7 models. Also: 147 genome-scale NextGen screens, biomarker dependencies, CDK6/CDKN2A-GBM work.
     8	2. **S2 (Sanger biobank):** 162-organoid dependency map, biomarker associations, organoid-specific essentials.
     9	3. **S3 (Oct 2026 transfer study):** expression-only Elastic Net (20 genes) transfer quantification 2D→organoids (Broad-94 ρ=0.15, Sanger-148 ρ=0.10), beyond-lineage checks. **Explicitly did not disentangle** state/geometry/medium/lineage/platform contributions. Generic transfer prediction is therefore closed as a novelty claim.
    10	4. Adjacent: murine MYC 3D-vs-2D fitness programs (PMC12055005, 2025) — different system, no human dependency-transfer estimand.
    11	
    12	## Residual uncertainty (the gap)
    13	Nobody has tested whether **measured** per-gene environmental sensitivity (paired medium/format deltas with proper joint uncertainty and heterogeneity) **explains which dependencies fail to transfer** — i.e., whether environment-labile genes are exactly where 2D→organoid relationships (dependency levels and/or biomarker associations) degrade, beyond lineage and baseline strength. S1 measured sensitivity (7 models, class-level report); S3 measured transfer failure (20 genes, no environmental attribution). The link, with correct independent-unit counting and heterogeneity-aware estimands, is unreported in the searched literature.
    14	
    15	Our audit adds a sharpening fact: per-gene average deltas hide extreme heterogeneity (91–98% of test genes flip sign across model pairs). Any credible claim must therefore be **conditional** (which genes × which model contexts) — class-average or mean-effect versions are likely unidentifiable/misleading at n=7 models. Precision-supported **refutations/bounds** (e.g., "average environmental sensitivity does not explain transfer degradation beyond X") are acceptable G1 targets.
    16	
    17	## Candidate claim ledger (exploratory; Lock 1 will bound the family)
    18	- **CL-1 (primary candidate):** Among the 250 panel genes, transfer degradation of dependency behavior (traditional→NextGen organoid, Layer-A genome-wide data) increases with measured environmental sensitivity magnitude/heterogeneity, conditional on lineage + baseline dependency strength. *Falsifier:* no association (or reversed) under joint-uncertainty propagation and strongest-baseline controls. *Needs:* Layer-A genome-wide import, joint bootstrap, matched controls.
    19	- **CL-2 (conditional form):** The CL-1 association is concentrated in specific gene classes/contexts (e.g., medium-labile lipid genes vs format-labile adhesion genes behave differently in transfer). *Risk:* n=7 models ⇒ classes, not genes, may be the finest honest resolution.
    20	- **CL-3 (bound/refutation candidate):** After conditioning, environmental sensitivity explains < δ of transfer degradation (informative bound redirecting interpretation toward lineage/state/platform). Qualifies only with predeclared δ + sufficient precision.
    21	- **Excluded as novelty:** re-deriving S1 class effects; re-running S3's 20-gene transfer; any average-effect "environment matters" statement without heterogeneity/conditioning.
    22	
    23	## Open items for G1 gate
    24	S1/S2 supplement + author-code deep read; Zenodo S3 code inspection; Layer-A genome-wide route decision; crosswalk completion; sol-high review of this audit + provenance (G1 gate requires ≥ 90).

cancer-dependency-rsi/reviews/g1_question_candidate.md
     1	# Gate G1 — candidate identifiable question (DRAFT for sol-high G1 review; not locked)
     2	
     3	## Named question (v1 candidate)
     4	**Within the Broad controlled environmental panel (7 models; 5 matched medium + 7 matched format contrasts; 250 selected genes) linked to Layer-A genome-wide traditional↔NextGen dependency data: does measured per-gene environmental sensitivity — magnitude and cross-model heterogeneity of paired medium/format deltas, propagated with donor/model-joint uncertainty — predict transfer degradation of dependency behavior from conventional models to organoids, beyond lineage, baseline dependency strength, and the strongest S3-style expression baseline?**
     5	
     6	## Falsifiable alternatives
     7	- H1 (conditional explanation): transfer degradation increases with environmental sensitivity in specific gene-class × context strata (medium-labile vs format-labile may differ), robust to joint-uncertainty propagation and ablation.
     8	- H0/bound: after conditioning, environmental sensitivity explains < δ (δ to be predeclared at Lock 1 from development precision) of transfer degradation — an informative bound redirecting interpretation to lineage/state/platform.
     9	- H-heterogeneity: only heterogeneity (sign-flipping across models), not mean magnitude, predicts degradation — implying model-context-specific rather than gene-intrinsic lability.
    10	
    11	## Identifiability basis (measured, 2026-10-28)
    12	- Paired contrasts exist at row level (manifested Chronos matrix 18×1000, 0 missing); direction integrity vs published OLS r=0.91/0.96 (test250).
    13	- Independent units: **7 biological models** for environmental estimand (donor mapping for these 7 to confirm in crosswalk; ACH-003052 lineage gap open). Genes/contrasts are not independent units.
    14	- Heterogeneity dominates (91–98% sign-flipping) ⇒ estimands are conditional/stratified; every claim reports all individual paired changes; no average-only claims.
    15	- Transfer side (Layer-A): genome-wide matrix route pending (portal file set vs figshare result tables — Lock 1 decision with leakage audit of Chronos pre-training). If genome-wide route fails, question narrows to the 250-gene panel using NextGen-vs-traditional published test tables (documented narrowing, broader component unmet).
    16	- Prior art excluded: S1 class-level format/medium effects (controls); S3 20-gene expression transfer (baseline to beat/explain, not the claim).
    17	
    18	## What G1 still needs
    19	Crosswalk completion (donors for the 7 models; HCM-SANG↔ACH), assay audit write-up, S3 Zenodo code inspection, sol-high review of provenance+novelty+this question ≥ 90. No adaptive search before G1 passes.

5 - Data and identifiability audit

Before adaptive hypothesis search, construct a provenance table with biological model, donor, tumor lineage, sample origin, laboratory, assay library, medium, growth format, replicate identity, release, and measured outcome.

Create explicit crosswalks among Broad, HCMI, Sanger, and author-specific identifiers. Mark uncertain donor identity as uncertain rather than assuming independence.

Determine which observations are truly paired. Count independent biological models and donors. Do not count genes, guide RNAs, wells, alternative cultures, or technical replicates as additional independent patients.

Audit release composition and preprocessing provenance. An integrated release may contain validation observations or corrections learned jointly from them. Removing overlapping rows alone does not undo leakage through outcome-guided target selection, normalization, or model selection. Prefer compatible study-specific releases and transformations fitted on development data. If a fitted correction used external dependency outcomes, rebuild a development-only analysis or obtain genuinely independent validation evidence. Disclosure alone does not restore independence. Distinguish that outcome leakage from covariate-only joint preprocessing, which must be disclosed as transductive feature preprocessing with appropriately limited claims. This is a required provenance check, not an assertion that any named release is contaminated.

Verify dependency score direction, dynamic range, controls, missingness, and quality flags separately for each source. Avoid direct subtraction of Chronos values from Sanger fold-change or essentiality scores.

Preserve the seven-model environmental design as a small, selected experiment. Determine whether row-level paired outcomes, replicates, and required metadata are sufficient for the intended contrast. A batch correction cannot separate laboratory and biology when the experimental design confounds them.

Record attrition explicitly, including models unable to grow in a condition and genes excluded for insufficient variation or precision.

Data eligibility is determined from prespecified quality and identifiability rules. It must not be selected later to maximize a favorable result.

The gate is an identifiable, falsifiable question in a named subset. If the data cannot support the original question, propose and document a narrower still-open question within the same scientific objective. Do not pretend the original broader goal has been achieved.


6 - Scientific estimands and research contract

Start from two connected estimands.

The direct environmental estimand is the change in a gene's measured dependency between environment A and environment B within the same biological model, while holding the other measured culture condition fixed. Analyze medium and growth format separately where the design permits.

The transfer estimand is the extent to which a prespecified genotype, tumor-state, or context relationship predicts dependency behavior in an independently filtered organoid cohort beyond lineage and the strongest appropriate baseline.

Use original documented assay units for paired within-study contrasts. Use justified ranking, calibration, or outcome-specific comparisons for cross-study evidence.

For each environmental claim, report the dependency in both conditions, all individual paired changes, uncertainty, and heterogeneity. A near-zero average can conceal large opposing effects.

Do not call a dependency invariant because a difference is nonsignificant. If claiming equivalence, define a scientifically justified equivalence margin before confirmatory evaluation and require the relevant interval to fall inside that margin. If no defensible margin exists, report continuous effects and uncertainty without an invariance label.

A stable absence of dependency is not a stable cancer vulnerability. Establish that the dependency itself is meaningful in the environments covered by a positive claim.

Do not interpret a predictive expression feature as a causal mediator without identifying assumptions and suitable evidence. Environmental intervention evidence and cross-cohort biomarker associations must retain distinct evidence labels.

Carry uncertainty and shared-model dependence in estimated environmental sensitivity into downstream transfer analyses using a justified measurement-error, resampling, or sensitivity analysis. Do not treat noisy estimated sensitivities as exact independent inputs. Report conclusions that change materially across plausible environmental-effect estimates.

During initial development, choose and justify the precise primary endpoint, practical effect margin, hypothesis family, baseline set, uncertainty procedure, and sensitivity analyses. Then freeze them before policy optimization.

The research contract must include the following.

- Exact question and falsifiable alternatives.
- Intended population, environments, targets, and generalization claim.
- Eligibility and exclusion rules.
- Data and identifier versions.
- Development, policy-evaluation, and final external partitions.
- Feature engineering and transformation rules.
- Primary and secondary endpoints with fixed direction.
- Minimum practical effects or equivalence margins and their justification.
- Multiplicity control and uncertainty methods.
- Baseline definitions.
- Invalid-result rules.
- Success, failure, inconclusive, and budget-exhaustion conditions.

The initial lock fixes the permitted hypothesis and transformation spaces, partitions, evaluator, baseline definitions, practical margins, and inferential rules. Adaptive development may select hypotheses, features, hyperparameters, and fitted artifacts within those spaces. A second lock commits the exact selected claim family, predictions, and artifacts before external evaluation.

Hash this contract, evaluator code, and split manifests. Changing a locked scientific contract creates a new study identifier and requires appropriate new evaluation evidence.


7 - Novelty and biological scope

Maintain a claim ledger throughout the project.

For each proposed claim, record the nearest prior result, which part is already established, the precise remaining uncertainty, evidence needed to resolve it, and the consequence if the claim is supported or refuted.

Read the Broad environmental analyses, the Sanger biomarker analyses, and the October transfer study closely. Their reported lipid, adhesion, state, and dependency findings are controls or prior evidence. Recovering them is not a new discovery.

Use current primary literature, author data, and official documentation. Date the search and preserve queries and important exclusions. Absence of an exact search hit is not proof that a claim is new.

Choose a bounded hypothesis family before final evaluation. Consider gene classes with measured environmental sensitivity and adequate overlap, not an unconstrained scan of every feature until a small p-value appears.

Before finalizing the manuscript, repeat the novelty audit for each surviving exact claim. Preserve contradictions and prior evidence. Do not silently rename a known association to make it appear new.

The required scientific contribution must be more specific than weak organoid transfer, better average correlation, or successful agent automation.


8 - Baselines, inference, and adversarial checks

Build simple competent baselines before allowing elaborate models.
     1	# Provenance & identifiability audit v1 (2026-10-28, Phase 1 — in progress)

     3	## Verified from primary full texts (Europe PMC fullTextXML, local copies in `data/papers/`)
     4	
     5	### S1 Broad NextGen (PMC13581591, doi:10.1038/s41586-026-10843-7)
     6	- 147 genome-scale CRISPR screens; NextGen organoids/spheroids; 10 cancer types; Chronos processing (incl. Chronos **pre-trained on traditional+NextGen CNS models** before organoid application — preprocessing-provenance flag for transfer analyses).
     7	- **Environmental minipool:** enAsCas12a library, **250 test genes** from 9+ functional classes (Integrin, ECM Adhesion, Actin Regulation/Nucleation, Adherens/Tight/Gap Junction, Lipid Metabolism, Wnt, PI3K-MTOR, Cell Cycle, Other per `minipool_library_composition.csv`); genes selected partly from 3D-vs-2D dependency differences → **selected, not representative**.
     8	- **18 screens, 7 biological models** (ACH-000048, ACH-000108, ACH-000178, ACH-001418, ACH-002958, ACH-003040, ACH-003052): format = 2D plastic vs 3D Matrigel dome; medium = RPMI/FBS (serum) vs OPAC (serum-free). Screen counts: dome 11, plastic 7, OPAC 9, RPMI-FBS 9. QC: total reads ≥ 5×10⁶, NNMD ≤ −1.5 (observed NNMD −9.19…−1.81, ROC-AUC 0.786…0.951). Analysis: Chronos + OLS with format, serum, interaction terms (`minipool_regression_coefficients.csv`).
     9	- **Published findings = CONTROLS (not discoveries):** integrin/actin-regulation classes vary by growth format; lipid-metabolism class varies by medium; differential integrin vs cholesterol-homeostasis effects.
    10	- Data: DepMap portal file set "NextGen Model Manuscript 2026"; figshare 10.6084/m9.figshare.29472362 (CC BY 4.0). Code: github.com/broadinstitute/DepMap-NextGen-Public. Raw WGS/RNA in dbGaP phs003444 (controlled — **not used**; processed public data only).
