    11	
    12	## 0. Operating rules (bind every phase)
    13	
    14	### 0.1 Model routing & the frozen generator protocol (user-mandated)
    15	| Role | Model | Effort | Pattern |
    16	|---|---|---|---|
    17	| Adversarial review of **everything** | `gpt-6.1-sol` | `high` | `codex exec --skip-git-repo-check -m gpt-6.1-sol -c model_reasoning_effort='"high"' -s read-only` |
    18	| Generation (candidates, challengers, summaries, extraction, unstick) | `gpt-6.1-sol` | `xhigh` | same, `xhigh`, sandbox per task |
    19	| Lead investigator / statistics / implementation | Host agent | — | Local; all computation local |
    20	
    21	- **Review protocol:** severity-tagged issues (low/medium/high/critical) with file:line evidence + one 0–100 score → lead verifies each (accept/reject with reason) → fix all verified → re-review until **≥ 90 and zero verified high/critical**. Transcripts + dispositions in `reviews/` + review ledger.
    22	- **Generator-protocol freeze (isolates policy improvement):** for any policy comparison, the *entire* generation protocol is frozen and versioned — proposer prompt, direction-summary prompt, catalog-extraction prompt+schema, candidate ranking inputs, repair/unstick allowances, model IDs + efforts. Fixed arms run the **same protocol on their own admissible histories**. Any discretionary intervention outside the frozen protocol is logged as a **protocol breach**. The primary comparison is **assignment-retentive**: all assigned attempts, failures, and costs stay in their arm (no post-hoc exclusion of inconvenient outcomes). Exclusions are allowed **only** under predeclared, outcome-independent criteria frozen before the comparison. A breach that changes admissible information or treatment **invalidates the affected comparison** (restart requires appropriately unused evaluation evidence); logging/charging the breach never repairs biased inclusion. Only the executable **policy module** differs between arms.
    23	- **Context/retrieval contract:** "exhaustive/full history" always means **exhaustive over the episode's admissible history** (§0.4.2 filter) — the same filter applies to *every* input channel: history index, retrieved records, rendered prompts, summaries, priorities, and unstick packets. Proposer inputs (token-bounded by construction; budgets frozen in the SPEC with reserved space for instructions + retrieved records): (a) **bounded directory header** — partition list with counts and ID ranges only (never a full ID dump), (b) **history index partitions** (fixed-size rows: ID, direction, origin, parents, outcome class, quality, cost), partitioned deterministically by direction and **sub-partitioned by fixed row/token caps so no partition — including one huge direction — exceeds its bound**, (c) episode-admissible catalog/frontier view, (d) direction summary, (e) full records via a **runner-mediated ID lookup** (proposer cites IDs or ID ranges; runner resolves partition→records deterministically, budgeted per generation, every lookup logged; cross-partition access always by ID). Complete addressability = every admissible ID is reachable via header→partition→lookup, without ever rendering the whole directory. **Overflow outcome:** if no legal rendering fits the frozen token budget, the generation call splits further (finer sub-partitions, header unchanged) — if still infeasible, the SPEC must narrow its index scope explicitly and the narrowing is ledgered; silent truncation is forbidden. Fixtures required: directory-alone-exceeds-cap and single-direction-exceeds-cap cases both rendering legally. What each call received is recorded in its input manifest. This is the single definition of "full tried history" used in GOAL.md. The hidden-outcome invariance test (§0.4.2) must cover the **complete rendered input and resulting decisions**, all channels included.
    24	- Specialist roles stay distinct (novelty, provenance, modeling, evaluator, adversarial review). The evaluator gets a narrower interface than the proposer — a logged protocol, **not** a cryptographic boundary, never described as one.
    25	- Every model call ledgered: model, effort, exact prompt (retained, §0.3), tokens when exposed, wall/CPU, peak RSS, cost or `unknown`. Additional metered API cap = **$0** unless user-configured; unmeasurable material cost ⇒ C9 all-in savings uncertifiable (Phase 5).
    26	
    27	### 0.2 Resource envelope & worst-case reservations (`config/resources.json`, enforced in code)
    28	- No GPU; ≤ 2 CPU-heavy jobs; research RSS ≤ min(8 GiB, 60% RAM) = **8 GiB**; exploratory timeout **600 s** (longer only via recorded campaign allocation); downloads ≤ **min(20 GiB, available_disk/4)** computed and persisted at each allocation (532 GiB free at start ⇒ 20 GiB); pilot = 24 nodes + 3 challengers + incumbent, ≤ 8 h. Ceilings never auto-increase.
    29	- **Allocations bound the campaign from Phase 0, not just after Lock 1:** Phase 0 creates `config/allocations.json` with (i) **pre-lock budgets** (bootstrap/audit wall time, model-call count, max review rounds per gate — default 5 rounds then escalation), (ii) **repeat-failure limits** (same blocker surviving 3 unstick rounds + 2 generation attempts ⇒ pre-lock stopping assessment, §0.6), (iii) the **terminal-review reserve** (pre-reserved sol-xhigh call budget, untouchable for other work), (iv) placeholders refined into full campaign allocations at Lock 1 (per-phase + total). Spending against allocations is ledgered from the first job.
    30	- **Reservation rules (spec §4, acceptance-tested):** before any dispatch, reserve the **conservative billable maximum** (not an estimate); concurrent reservations and **retry reservations count** against the cap; the reservation record is **persisted+fsynced before dispatch**; interrupted ⇒ stays `reserved` until reconciled against returned usage; **never dispatch to a provider whose billable maximum cannot be bounded** by config; completed calls never repeated on resume. Tests must cover concurrent requests, retries, and interrupted reservations. (Local compute jobs reserve wall/RSS slots the same way with $0 cost.)
    31	
    32	### 0.3 Persistence, provenance retention & honesty primitives
    33	- Append-only JSONL ledgers (`experiments`, `resources`, `reviews`, `claims`); node states `proposed → dispatched → completed|failed → committed`; checkpoint after every completed experiment.
    34	- **Retained provenance (hashes are indexes, not substitutes):** a content-addressed artifact store (`artifacts/sha256/…`) retains, immutably, for every node: artifact bytes; **exact rendered prompt**; **canonical generator-input manifest** (every input artifact ID+hash, incl. parent artifacts, catalog/summary/frontier snapshot IDs, budget state, rejection history shown); **action/observation event sequence** (policy inputs/outputs in order); selecting **policy ID+version+state**; generator model+effort+seed; data/split/evaluator hashes **and versions**; execution-environment manifest (env lock hash, host fingerprint); result, validity diagnostics, failures, measured costs.
    35	- `STATUS.md` + `status.json`: phase, incumbent, spend, three separate status fields (`scientific_discovery`, `prediction_improvement`, `policy_improvement`), unmet C1–C13 gates, next action, exact resume command. "Running" claimed only with live PID/job + heartbeat.
    36	- Terminal states only from GOAL. Failed final test stays failed; reuse of its outcomes = exploratory.
    37	
    38	### 0.4 Trajectory system (Dream-RSI adaptation — departures disclosed)
    39	
    40	Adapted from Dream-RSI (arXiv:2609.14858; `google-research/dream-rsi`): discovery history as replay simulator; offline policy improvement ("dreaming") then online redeployment; fixed generator+evaluator+task, only policy changes. Spec constraints override the paper where they conflict; then the method is labeled **Dream-RSI-inspired adaptation**.
    41	
    42	**0.4.1 Units & DAG schema.** *Node/attempt* = one evaluated artifact. Every node has an **ordered parent array** (possibly empty, one, or many), forming an **acyclic** lineage DAG validated at insert. Each parent edge records the parent's immutable contribution (which components carried over). Nodes carry `study_id`, `evaluator_version`, `world_id` (the round/tree that produced them; recombination children belong to their birth world and reference parents across worlds by ID). **Replay eligibility:** a node is replayable in an episode only if (a) every information-bearing parent is either revealed earlier in the episode or in the initially-available set, (b) study/evaluator versions match the episode, and (c) its canonical input manifest matches the episode state (§0.4.6). **Ancestry cost** = cost of the union of required ancestor nodes, charged **once** per episode (Phase 5 allocation). *Branch/trajectory* = the primary-parent (first-parent) chain view of the DAG, used for best-so-far curves; *discovery tree* = one round's forest under one policy on one panel, frozen+hashed into the **trace pool** as a replay world (pool only grows). Tree shape `BRANCHES × (REFINE+1)` scaled to budget; exact values frozen at Lock 1. *Direction* = coherent research line over many nodes.
    43	
    44	*Identities (dedup done right, arm-scoped):* `attempt_id` · `context_hash` · `config_id` · `evaluation_id` (config × data × split × evaluator version). **Duplicate eligibility is computed only against the acting arm's episode-admissible history**, scoped by (study, panel, split): the same `config_id` under a new `evaluation_id` (genuinely new evidence/panel) is legal under the frozen symmetric-evaluation rule; re-running an existing `evaluation_id` *within the same arm scope* is a replication (prespecified reason required). The global artifact store retains bytes for audit, but **global membership never changes any arm's observations, rejections, or legal actions** (no existence-oracle across arms/episodes). Required tests: adding hidden/other-arm configs must not change an arm's behavior; reversing arm execution order must not change either arm's outcomes. Trial accounting uses these levels (§0.5.1.8).
    45	
    46	**0.4.2 Direction catalog with episode views.** `directions/catalog.json` + immutable snapshots (`history/iterNNN.json`). Status is **four independent fields**, each with deterministic predicates in a **total transition table frozen at Lock 1** (every state combination + missing-evidence + precedence + recovery transitions defined; each transition cites evidence node IDs): `depth` ∈ {unexplored, underexplored, explored, well_explored} · `promise` ∈ {unknown, unpromising, promising} · `fragility` ∈ {unknown, robust, fragile} · `recoverability` ∈ {n/a, recoverable, dead}. Derived labels (e.g. "saturated" = depth well_explored + marginal-gain < ε over last k attempts) are **resource-allocation judgments, never scientific exhaustion verdicts**. Catalog also stores: evidence attempt IDs, best/median quality, quality-vs-cost slope, failure taxonomy, `avoid_repeating`, bottlenecks. Extraction (sol-xhigh, schema-validated) proposes classifications; **deterministic predicates verify them** against recorded outcomes — mismatches rejected+ledgered.
    47	**Episode views (no future leakage):** anything fed to a policy/generator during replay — catalog snapshot, frontier, summaries, priorities, legal actions — is **recomputed from admissible history only**: complete earlier worlds + the revealed prefix of the current world. Global/current catalog objects never enter replay. Acceptance test: mutating any unrevealed outcome must not change any episode-view artifact or policy decision (hidden-outcome invariance test).
    48	
    49	**0.4.3 Trajectory scoring (formal, deterministic, no LLM).** Locked at Lock 1 with formulas; defaults: per-node **valid quality** `q ∈ [0,1]` = locked monotone transform of the primary scientific endpoint (higher = better; sign/direction fixed in contract; invalid ⇒ no q, counted invalid, never 0-as-quality). Per trajectory with cumulative measured cost `c`: best-valid-so-far `B(c) = max q over valid nodes with cumulative cost ≤ c` (0 if none); **cost-to-threshold** `C(τ) = min c with B(c) ≥ τ`, else **censored at budget** (never an invented number); **normalized area-over-curve** `AoC = (1/B_ep) ∫₀^{B_ep} B(c) dc` over the episode budget `B_ep`; validity rate; **robustness** `R` = locked sensitivity statistic (e.g. min quality retained across prespecified sensitivity replicates, formula at Lock 1). **Pareto frontier** computed **within a comparable domain** (same task panel + evaluator version + endpoint) over (final B, total cost, R). Precedence: frontier membership = evidence quality; saturation/pruning = allocation status — a nondominated-but-saturated trajectory stays frontier-listed but allocation-paused; neither label overwrites the other.
    50	
    51	**0.4.4 Origins & generation.** Sol-xhigh proposes candidates under the frozen protocol (§0.1); each declares exactly one node-operation/origin:
    52	- `new` (0 parents; opens unexplored direction) · `refinement` (exactly 1 parent; mutate/extend a frontier or winner artifact) · `underexplored_extension` (1+ parents in a thin-evidence direction) · `combination` (≥2 parents; each contribution stated; legality of each element checked; revalidated from scratch; no score inheritance) · `repair` (≥1 parent + cited failure node ID + named fix).
    53	Runner rejects (ledgered, fed back next round): outside locked spaces; duplicate `config_id` without replication authorization; nonexistent parents/evidence; `avoid_repeating` repeats without `repair` justification; cyclic or version-incompatible lineage.
    54	
    55	**0.4.5 Selection = executable policy over prefix-recomputed state.** Proposal (sol-xhigh: what *could* run + priorities as inputs) ≠ selection (versioned `policy(episode_view, legal_actions, remaining_budget) → action`). **Legal actions are recomputed at each step from the revealed prefix** using frozen transition/pruning rules — historical legal-action sequences are audit records, never counterfactual state for challengers. Keep/prune: frontier trajectories refinable; allocation-paused (saturated) directions leave the legal set until a frozen reactivation rule fires; `fragile` trajectories refinable only via prespecified robustness diagnostics. Deterministic recomputation covers all code-computed state. **LLM-produced state** (direction summaries, rankings, proposals, extraction text) is **immutable memoized output**: stored content-addressed, keyed by (canonical admissible-state hash, generator-protocol version); replay/offline reconstruction **reads the memo — never re-invokes the model**. Cache miss during replay ⇒ that transition is `unsupported` (online execution may create the memo, charged once at creation, then reused). Required test: repeated reconstruction from the same revealed outcomes yields byte-identical rendered inputs with zero additional model calls. Catalog *classifications* remain predicate-verified deterministically (§0.4.2); free-text summaries/rankings are memo-replayed, not regenerated.
    56	
    57	**0.4.6 Dreaming: replay objective, ρ, exact context, common support.**
    58	- **ρ is a budget-control parameter** (as in the paper): replay evaluates each policy at operating budgets `ρ·B_ep` for ρ ∈ {0, 0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 1}. **Replay objective (complete formula frozen at Lock 1; default):** per episode e and ρ, `J_e(π,ρ) = B_e(ρ·B_ep)` (best valid quality attained within that budget; invalid nodes contribute cost, no quality; zero-attainment ⇒ 0). Policy score `J(π) = Σ_ρ w_ρ · mean_e J_e(π,ρ)` over the **common eligible episode set** (below) with locked weights `w_ρ` (default uniform). **Total ordering (frozen before replay; no residual ties):** (1) higher J; (2) higher attainment fraction `P(B_e(B_ep) ≥ τ)`; (3) lower **restricted-mean cost-to-τ** `mean_e min(C_e(τ), B_ep)` (identifiable under censoring); (4) earlier position in the SPEC's pre-listed candidate order; (5) lexicographically smaller immutable candidate ID. The incumbent participates as an ordinary candidate in this ordering; "retain incumbent" is simply the outcome when the incumbent orders first — never an extra discretionary step. Costs are measured ledger costs (§0.4.1 ancestry rule); endpoints' signs/scales per contract.
    59	- **Exact context (operational; history-dependent, not "parent-local"):** refinement generation is **history-dependent exact-context replay** — the generator's inputs are exactly its canonical manifest (parent artifact(s) + frozen instruction + episode-view snapshot ID + budget token + retrieved records). A recorded child replays **iff** the challenger's episode state reproduces that manifest hash exactly; **any differing input ⇒ `unsupported`**, never approximated. Before any screening reliance, Phase 3/4 must **measure attainable support on reordered traces** (including context-overflow and budget-token variation) and report it; if attainable support fails §below thresholds, replay screening is inadmissible by construction for that comparison.
    60	- **Common support (frozen at Lock 1; no policy-specific deletion):** for each ρ separately, an episode is **fully supported for policy π at ρ** if every decision π requests within budget `ρ·B_ep` replays as supported. The **common eligible set at ρ** = episodes fully supported for **every** compared policy at that ρ (intersection; fixed episode-level denominator = the frozen episode list size, never trajectory-length-dependent). `J_e` means are computed **only on the common eligible set, identically for all policies** — policies are never ranked on different surviving sets. **Adequacy:** if the common set at any ρ with `w_ρ > 0` falls below the locked threshold (default ≥ 8 episodes **and** ≥ 50% of the frozen list), the screen is **inadmissible** for that comparison → fresh rollouts decide (§Phase 4 fallback). Unsupported actions terminate that policy's episode copy as `unsupported` (removing the episode from the common set for everyone at that ρ, reported per-policy as the cause) and can **never** count as cheap successful stops. Coverage (per policy, per ρ) always reported beside scores.
    61	- Both arms share the same first round **E0** (starting policy; copied read-only; its production cost charged per Phase 5 allocation — copying is not free).
    62	
    63	**0.4.7 Audit views.** `status --trajectories`: lineage DAG (all parents), direction board (4-field statuses), frontier data, generation-over-generation table, allocation-paused list + reasons, coverage reports. Derived deterministically from ledgers; ledger wins on disagreement (= view bug).
    64	
    65	### 0.5 Generation protocol (adapted from the business LOOP, hardened)
    66	
    67	Modeled on `~/bufferright/business/data/research/paths/LOOP/` (48 generations; "Sol xhigh proposes, Sol high reviews"; score trails 54→76→92). Translation: sleeve pool → analysis-family pool; blend → `combination`; PREREG hash → SPEC hash; forward ledger → frozen predictions; anchor → evaluator-compatibility suite (below). **Two separate lifecycles** (never mixed): **(S) scientific-artifact generations** and **(P) policy generations**, each with own SPEC, screen, battery, denominators, trial counters.
    68	
    69	**0.5.1 Anatomy (`generations/{s,p}genNNN/`, never reused/overwritten).**
    70	1. **SPEC.md + SHA-256 locked in ledger before any run:** discriminating question, candidate families, screen definition (incl. whether it is a *necessary-condition* screen — §0.5.2), battery definition, pass bar, stop rules, reserved battery budget. Amendments = new versioned files; locked-science amendments follow new-study-ID rule.
    71	2. **Enumerate** from the pool under frozen protocol; origins+parents per §0.4.4; legality validated.
    72	3. **Screen (ranking only, labeled approximate).** Screen-positives are never claims.
    73	4. **Recombine/refine winners** (`combination`/`refinement`); re-enter at screen; no score inheritance.
    74	5. **Battery (decisive):** (S) full frozen evaluator + validity + matched controls + ablations on fresh development rollouts; (P) Phase 4/5 procedure (replay screen → fresh paired rollouts incl. incumbent). Battery budget is **reserved before screening**; if ceilings intervene, unfinished batteries are `PENDING`, never silently dropped.
    75	6. **Verdicts are scoped to what was tested:** `ADVANCES` · `FAILS-BATTERY (tested finalists: n/N, with numbers)` · `FAMILY-CLOSED` (allowed **only** with a proven necessary-condition screen or exhaustive battery — §0.5.2; else untested members remain explicitly open) · `INVALID` (defective record preserved, corrected in a new version) · `BLOCKED-DATA` (no proxy substitution without claim downgrade) · `PENDING`.
    76	7. **Sol-high review → verify → fix → re-review to ≥ 90**, per-issue dispositions (`CONFIRMED+FIXED` / `ACCEPTED with claim downgrade` / `REJECTED with evidence`).
    77	8. **`GENERATIONS.md` entry:** numbers for every finalist incl. failures, lessons, next mandate, stop-rule checks (leakage ⇒ dependent lines cancelled), and **multi-counter trial accounting** (cumulative, separate: unique configs, attempts, replications, hypothesis inspections, policy candidates, replay episodes, batteries — scientific vs engineering adaptation never merged into one total; no count substitutes for confirmatory procedure).
    78	
    79	**0.5.2 Screening honesty.** A screen justifies exhaustive rejection **only** if passing it is a mathematically necessary condition for passing the battery (proof stated in SPEC, e.g. battery metric is a monotone refinement bounded by the screen metric). Otherwise verdicts read "no *tested finalist* passes" and screen-negative members stay listed as untested. Cheapest-falsification probes run as one fixed SPEC with no variants; failure closes only the tested specification, with family-level closure requiring the necessary-condition argument.
    80	
    81	**0.5.3 Evaluator versioning (anchor is not certification).** Scores from different evaluator versions are **never pooled** in one comparison/replay pool. The frozen baseline "anchor" reproduction is a smoke check only. Any evaluator change requires: compatibility suite (rankings + validity decisions on a frozen probe set), explicit invalidation list for affected results, and — if substantive — the new-study/new-evidence procedure. Versions preserved side-by-side.
    82	
    83	**0.5.4 Matched controls & post-hoc rule.** A new ingredient is credited only vs a same-construction control lacking it; unmatched construction changes are disclosed as non-decomposable. Post-hoc discoveries (e.g. control beats hypothesis) are flagged post-hoc, may be frozen for forward judging, never promotable on consumed data.
    84	
    85	**0.5.5 Generation boundary (end-to-end).** Deterministic scores/frontier/episode views → sol-xhigh direction summary + ranked proposal (keep / recombine-with-rationale / repair / open / pause, all ID-cited) → policy code executes selection under budget → runs → extraction + predicate verification → sol-high ≥ 90 → ledger + trace-pool append.
    86	
    87	### 0.6 Autonomy, unstick protocol, campaign limits & terminal determination
    88	- **Default = continue autonomously** through failures/negative results, generation after generation, **within campaign allocations** (Lock 1: per-phase + total wall/nodes/model-calls, inside §0.2 ceilings). Legitimate stops: hard authorization gate (credential/spending/required file), allocation/ceiling exhaustion, explicit user stop, no-progress determination (below), terminal determination.
    89	- **Unstick (sol-xhigh):** on stall (no obvious legal action, unexpected gate failure, all directions paused/dead/blocked, contradictory results, same failure twice) invoke sol-xhigh with the state packet (STATUS, episode views, frontier, last 3 generation entries, blocker + attempts) for ranked unblocks + cheapest discriminating experiment + draft next SPEC. Verify vs locked contract (sol cannot authorize contract/ceiling/final-access/standard changes); ledger consultation+disposition; **unstick calls are charged costs** (Phase 5). Max 3 consultation rounds per blocker, then smallest legal evidence-gathering action. Under a frozen comparison, unstick input is restricted to that arm's admissible history (§0.1 freeze).
    90	- **No-progress / negative-EV rule (prevents indefinite retries, spec §14):** applies **before Lock 1 too**, against §0.2 pre-lock allocations: a pre-lock blocker (crosswalk, pairing, review gate) surviving its repeat-failure limit, or K consecutive generations (K default 3, finalized at Lock 1) with no frontier movement / new identifiable question / gate progress, triggers joint sol-xhigh + lead assessment; concurrence ⇒ admissible **non-complete terminal determination** (`INCONCLUSIVE`/`BLOCKED`) with unmet gates + smallest next step. Gate reviews exceeding the Phase-0 round cap escalate to the user with the residual issue list rather than looping.
    91	- **Terminal determination (sol-xhigh judges, evidence rules):** `COMPLETE_*` requires sol-xhigh's written gate-by-gate (C1–C13) certification over the full evidence package, score ≥ 90, zero unresolved high/critical — **in addition to** the evidence, never instead of it; it cannot waive gates. A **pre-reserved terminal-review budget slice** (Lock 1) guarantees the call is affordable; if it still cannot run, the executor may mechanically checkpoint and report `BLOCKED`/`BUDGET_EXHAUSTED` honestly — but **positive completion always requires the sol-xhigh determination**. Non-complete determinations list exact unmet gates; consumed Layer-B/C evidence stays consumed (Phases 5–6).
    92	
    93	---
    94	
    95	## Phase 0 — Bootstrap (Gate G0)
    96	Create `cancer-dependency-rsi/` (README, GOAL copy+hash, PROTOCOL stub, STATUS+status.json, `config/{resources,host}.json`, `src/`, `data/`, `ledger/`, `reviews/`, `artifacts/`, `generations/`, `directions/`, `trace_pool/`, `tests/`, `manuscript/`); project `.venv` via uv + lockfile; implement first: budget wrapper (§0.2 tests incl. concurrency/retries/interruption), append-only ledgers, artifact store, hashing, CLI skeleton (`bootstrap, audit-data, reproduce-baselines, run-pilot, run-cycle, evaluate-policy, lock-science, evaluate-external, reproduce, status, resume` — gate-locked commands fail loudly naming the gate). Record host fingerprint without secrets. **G0:** status/resume work; dummy over-budget job demonstrably killed (timeout+RSS); tests green; sol-high ≥ 90. *(Status 2026-10-28: scaffold + ledger/budget + 3 tests done; CLI + remaining tests pending.)*
    97	
    98	## Phase 1 — Quarantine-first verification & identifiability audit (Gate G1 → C1–C3)
    99	**1a. Metadata-only first.** Before importing any outcome-bearing file: create **provisional A/B/C partitions from metadata alone** (model/donor/lineage lists, no outcome values), write the **quarantine + exposure log** (every outcome-file access records who/what/when/which partition). Permitted pre-Lock-1 outcome contact: **Layer-A (development) sources only** (incl. the Broad minipool + genome-wide development data) and *published* QC summaries for B/C candidates. Sanger outcome matrices, once imported, sit quarantined as Layer-C candidates: schema/shape/control-gene inventory checks allowed via logged scripts; **no outcome-value inspection, ranking, or modeling** until Lock 2. Any accidental exposure is logged and downgrades affected claims.
   100	**1b. Read primary sources in full** (S1 PMC13581591, S2 PMC13581617, S3 PMC13664187 + Zenodo 22472622, S5 arXiv:2609.14858 + repo): exact designs, selection rules, score definitions/directions, QC, published findings (= controls). *(Done at full-text level 2026-10-28; supplements/code deep-read continues.)*
   101	**1c. Routes via official manifests only** — source URL, release/commit, date, license, bytes, SHA-256 per file into `data/source_manifest*.json`. Never invent URLs; never bypass challenges (DepMap portal verification page ⇒ use study-specific figshare releases, preferred anyway). **Leakage rule (spec §5, corrected):** if any correction/normalization/**target or model selection** used external outcomes, that evidence requires **development-only reconstruction or genuinely independent validation — disclosure never repairs it**; otherwise the independence gate stays **unmet**. Only **covariate-only** joint preprocessing may proceed as disclosed transductive preprocessing with limited claims. Audit Chronos pre-training + 26Q3 integration under this rule.
   102	**1d. Provenance + crosswalk + counts:** model/donor/lineage/origin/lab/library/medium/format/replicate/release/outcome; Broad↔HCMI↔Sanger↔author IDs; uncertain donor = `uncertain`; count **independent models/donors** (genes/guides/wells/cultures ≠ patients); attrition registry. Verify seeds at row level. *(Minipool verified: 7 models, 18 screens, 5 medium + 7 format matched contrasts, 250 selected genes, 0 missing; ACH-003052 lineage gap + HCM-SANG↔ACH crosswalk open.)*
   103	**1e. Assay audit (Layer-A + published summaries):** Chronos vs Sanger LFC/BAGEL — direction, range, controls, missingness, QC per source; absolute values never interchanged/subtracted. Confirm required pairings exist before modeling; check lab↔biology confounding.
   104	**1f. Novelty audit v1:** dated search (queries/exclusions preserved) + claim ledger (nearest prior, established part, precise residual uncertainty, evidence needed, consequences). Rule out generic 2D→organoid transfer (S3) and published format/medium class findings (S1) as novelty.
   105	**Gate G1:** named falsifiable question in a named subset + counted independent units + documented paired contrasts (or documented narrower question; broader component recorded unmet). No adaptive search before G1. Sol-high ≥ 90 on provenance + novelty package.
   106	
   107	## Phase 2 — Vertical slice, baselines, Lock 1 (Gate G2 → C4–C5)
   108	1. **Estimators (Layer-A only):** paired within-model contrasts in original units — both conditions, **all individual paired changes**, uncertainty, heterogeneity. **Joint uncertainty is mandatory with the correct independent unit:** the resampling unit is selected from the crosswalk — **donors** (resampling a donor carries *all* its nested models, cultures, contrasts, and every gene's paired vector together) whenever multiple models share a donor; models only when donor≡model is verified. Uncertain donor identity ⇒ conservative grouping (possibly-shared treated as shared) **plus** a sensitivity analysis under the split assumption; both reported. An explicit covariance model may substitute only with justification. Downstream transfer analyses refit per replicate; conclusions that flip are reported. Per-gene independent error bars alone are non-conforming. Effective sample sizes (donors *and* models) reported; few clusters ⇒ justify or downgrade intervals/tests. Tests include nested-donor fixtures.
   109	2. **Baselines** (same rows/info; preprocessing/selection/tuning inside development folds only): lineage-only; genotype-only; state/expression-only; genotype+state; +context/assay; regularized-linear à la S3 (from Zenodo code where possible). Compare vs **strongest**; prespecified environmental ablation; matched controls for any new ingredient. Final-outcome reproductions deferred to post-Lock-2. **Fold construction matches the generalization claim (blocked evaluation, spec §9):** fold manifests are frozen artifacts; for unseen-donor prediction, **every conservatively-linked donor's models, cultures, and repeated observations sit in exactly one fold — including nested tuning folds** (donor-grouped CV, same conservative grouping as §Phase 2.1); lineage/target/pathway blocking added where the claim requires it. Row-level random folds are non-conforming for donor-generalization claims. (Within-model paired environmental contrasts are a separate design, not CV folds, and remain governed by §Phase 2.1.) Required fixture: distinct model IDs sharing one donor must never straddle folds — build fails otherwise.
   110	3. **Evaluator v1** (separate module, narrow interface): effect sizes + calibrated predictive/uncertainty endpoints; **never** p-value size / discovery count / narrative confidence. Invalid rules: leakage, support violations, and **verified orientation/preprocessing errors** ⇒ `invalid`. A correctly measured **opposite-direction result is valid evidence** (potential refutation/heterogeneity), never invalidated for direction alone.
   111	4. **Lock 1 — freeze contract** (`PROTOCOL.md` + `contract.json`, hashed with evaluator + splits + generator protocol). Mandatory fields: question + falsifiable alternatives; population/environments/targets/generalization; eligibility; data/ID versions; **final A/B/C partitions** (blocked, overlap-recorded, refining Phase-1a provisionals using metadata only); feature/transform rules; endpoints (primary/secondary, fixed direction, q-transform §0.4.3); **meaningful-dependency-strength requirement for every positive vulnerability claim** (predeclared threshold in each claimed environment, tested independently of the environmental-change criterion; stable non-dependency ≠ vulnerability); practical-effect/equivalence margins + justification (no margin ⇒ no "invariant" label); multiplicity (default Holm FWER 0.05, small family) + joint-uncertainty method; baselines; invalid rules; success/failure/inconclusive/exhaustion; **trajectory machinery** (BRANCHES/REFINE, status transition table, replay objective + ρ weights, support thresholds, episode sets); **policy-comparison design** (Phase 5: resource endpoint fully specified, # independent blocks, savings estimand, paired estimator, censoring-compatible CI, precision-feasibility result, cost-allocation rules); **campaign allocations + retry/no-progress limits + terminal-review reserve**; context/retrieval contract. Change ⇒ new study ID + new evidence.
   112	5. **Tests:** leakage (donor overlap A/B/C fails build), score direction, grouping, synthetic-estimator behavior (code only), cost/reservation suite, resume idempotence, unsupported-replay, **hidden-outcome invariance** (§0.4.2), DAG acyclicity/version compat, final-outcome access guard + exposure log, evaluator-compatibility probe set.
   113	**Gate G2:** baselines reproduced on Layer-A (direction/cohort/measurement integrity; discrepancies bounded in writing); all hashes committed. Sol-high ≥ 90. **No policy optimization before G2.** *(Precursor measured: paired-mean vs published coefficients r = 0.91 format / 0.96 medium on test250.)*
   114	
   115	## Phase 3 — Pilot E0 + Replay World v1 (Gate G3, interim)
   116	Run the hand-designed **fixed exploration policy** (executable module; legal actions: open family / refine / diagnostic / stop; never labels/splits/metrics/margins) on Layer-A tasks to **24 evaluated nodes** under the frozen generator protocol. Freeze trees as **E0 / Replay World v1** + support-coverage report. **G3 issues an interim pilot report only** (verified data, identifiable question, baselines, real nodes, replay support, measured costs, next discriminating experiment) with policy comparison explicitly **unperformed** — G3 does **not** establish C13. Sol-high ≥ 90.
   117	
   118	## Phase 4 — Policy-improvement cycles (Gate G4 → C8, C13)
   119	Per cycle (≥ 2 genuine cycles; retained incumbent is valid), executed as (P)-generations (§0.5):
   120	1. Generate **incumbent + ≥ 3 executable challengers** (sol-xhigh code rewrites under frozen protocol; reflections without changed decisions don't count).
   121	2. **Replay-screen** all on identical frozen episode sets (§0.4.6 rules). **Selection/fallback is pre-registered in the (P)-SPEC before replay runs:** if the screen is admissible → fresh-test the top-1 challenger by J (ties per §0.4.6); if inadmissible → fresh-test challengers in the SPEC's pre-listed order (recorded before any replay output exists) until the cycle's frozen fresh-rollout allocation is spent. Every challenger's disposition is ledgered (`FRESH-TESTED` / `NOT-TESTED: screen-rank` / `NOT-TESTED: allocation`) — no discretionary post-hoc picking.
   122	3. **Fresh paired rollouts:** each fresh-tested challenger vs **incumbent** vs fixed exploration (same generator protocol per arm on own histories, evaluator, ceilings, initial artifacts, information; assignment-retentive inclusion §0.1). **Promotion requires beating the incumbent** by the locked improvement/noninferiority rule — beating fixed exploration alone is insufficient; replay superiority never substitutes.
   123	4. Promote or **retain incumbent**; failures kept; append Layer-A histories only; new world version. First completed fresh comparison (or its documented blocker) triggers the **first substantive cycle report** and is the earliest C13 evidence.
   124	**Gate G4:** ≥ 2 cycles ledgered end-to-end (challengers, coverage, paired rollouts, decisions+reasons). Sol-high ≥ 90/cycle. Inadequate replay support ⇒ preserved finding + "Dream-RSI-inspired adaptation" label with departures.
   125	
   126	## Phase 5 — Reserved policy evaluation (Gate G5 → C9)
   127	Freeze best admissible policy + selection criterion **before** touching Layer B. Arms: learned vs fixed exploration vs fixed-allocation/random — identical generator protocol (per-arm histories), evaluator version, artifacts, permissions, ceilings; donor/model/gene/pathway-blocked panels (gene splits of the 250 ≠ independent tasks; engineering-cohort → 7-model transfer explicitly bounded).
   128	- **Primary resource endpoint (fully specified at Lock 1):** named resource + units (default USD total measured cost; if an alternative is chosen: justification + its own practical margin + uncertainty criterion + measure-or-strictly-match all other material resources with tolerances); scalarization/aggregation across blocks; quality threshold τ (from development, attainable by baselines); **savings estimand + paired estimator + censoring-compatible interval** (non-attainment = censored, never invented costs); **# independent blocks + precision-feasibility check done at Lock 1** (infeasible precision ⇒ C9 recorded unachievable-by-design before optimization, not discovered after).
   129	- **Ambitious bar (default endpoint):** ≥ 20% savings vs strongest fixed baseline with **one-sided 95% lower bound ≥ 20%** (point > 20% with LB merely > 0 = some improvement, not C9). Report quality-vs-cost curves; end-to-end (incl. learning) and deployment-only views.
   130	- **Cost allocation (frozen, reconciled to resource ledger):** every component charged — generation, evaluation, replay, extraction, summaries, reviews, unstick, failed jobs, promotion trials; E0 + ancestry (§0.4.1) + shared artifacts allocated by the locked rule with stated deployment count (no silent amortization); **information produced in earlier worlds is not free**: each arm is charged the production cost of every history it reads (its own worlds at their recorded costs; shared E0 per the locked split), and both arms receive symmetric *access rules* to earlier-world information (own admissible history only, §0.4.2) — no arm may consume another arm's outcomes at any price. Memoized LLM state (§0.4.5) charged once at creation to its producing arm. Fixed baselines may spend equivalent budget on search. Reported totals must reconcile line-by-line with `ledger/resources.jsonl`.
   131	- **Measurability:** material unmeasurable host-model cost ⇒ C9 `unresolved_cost_gate` (no CPU/token proxy certification). Seeds = execution variability, not biological replication.
   132	- **Consumption:** after evaluation, **B panels are consumed**. Any further adaptation informed by B results (incl. via summaries/reviews) makes later B claims exploratory; a new confirmatory policy claim requires **new independent B evidence**.
   133	**Gate G5:** comparison + uncertainty complete; C9 met / not-met / unresolved with evidence. Sol-high ≥ 90.
   134	
   135	## Phase 6 — Lock 2 & single external evaluation (Gate G6 → C6, C10)
   136	1. **Lock 2 (`lock-science`):** exact bounded claim family, predictions, artifacts, inferential code + hashes. **Component-level evidence status attached to every claim** (and later every atlas decision): which dataset validates which component; adaptively reused environmental evidence labeled **exploratory or justified selection-adjusted** (adjustment stated) — external transfer success + Holm never retroactively confirms a selected environmental effect. Claims needing independent environmental confirmation require a suitable independent public controlled-environment dataset, else narrowed or gate-unmet (never gene-splits of the same 7 models). **Causal-interpretation gate (spec §6):** causal/mediation language ("explains *why* transfer fails", mediator claims for expression/state features) requires, per claim, a stated causal estimand + identifying assumptions + evidence suitable under those assumptions; otherwise language is constrained to (i) intervention effects actually measured in the paired environmental panel and (ii) cross-cohort predictive associations — "explanatory finding" in GOAL means this bounded, identified sense, never mechanism-by-association. The gate is applied at Lock 1 (claim templates) and rechecked at Lock 2 + manuscript.
   137	2. **Evaluate once** on Layer C (independently filtered Sanger cohort post-crosswalk/provenance/assay/representation checks; rank/calibration-appropriate cross-study comparisons). Wording: **retrospective** external validation; pretraining/literature exposure recorded.
   138	3. **Prespecified adversarial battery:** locked multiplicity; **dependency-strength gate** (Phase 2) for every positive claim; invariance only with positive dependency + interval-inside-margin equivalence; donor/lineage/platform sensitivity; leave-one-model-out; negative controls; leakage checks; grouping-respecting permutations; environmental ablation; joint-uncertainty fragility report.
   139	**Gate G6:** each claim supported/refuted/inconclusive under frozen rules; failed stays failed. Sol-high adversarial ≥ 90 (tasked to kill claims).
   140	
   141	## Phase 7 — Atlas, manuscript, reproduction, determination (Gate G7 → C7, C11–C12)
   142	- **Evidence atlas** (machine-readable): every eligible panel gene — dependency in both conditions, environmental change, uncertainty, heterogeneity, biomarker + transfer evidence, prior knowledge, **component evidence status**, missingness; every exclusion + reason; beyond-panel = exploratory.
   143	- **Manuscript:** estimands, provenance, both locks, baselines, all results incl. failures/retained incumbents, limitations (7 models, selected genes, assay non-interchangeability, retrospective validation, cost measurability), terminal state + unmet gates. No clinical claims from screens. **Dated claim-specific novelty refresh at G7** (and again at terminal determination) — informs C1/C6 currency without revising consumed confirmatory tests.
   144	- **Clean reproduction (`reproduce`):** fresh env from lockfile regenerates estimates/figures/atlas/policy comparison within tolerances from frozen artifacts, **no stochastic LLM rerun** → `reproducibility_report.md`.
   145	- **Terminal determination** per §0.6 (sol-xhigh, gate-by-gate C1–C13). Both `COMPLETE_*` states require all gates incl. C9; else honest state + unmet list.
   146	**Gate G7:** reproduction green; package per §8; sol-high ≥ 90 on manuscript/atlas/report.

     1	"""Phase-1 identifiability audit of the Broad minipool environmental panel.
     2	
     3	Builds matched within-model contrasts (medium holding format fixed; format
     4	holding medium fixed) from Chronos gene effects in original units, and
     5	checks direction agreement with the authors' published regression
     6	coefficients. Descriptive audit only -- no adaptive search, no claims.
     7	"""
     8	from __future__ import annotations
     9	
    10	import json
    11	from pathlib import Path
    12	
    13	import numpy as np
    14	import pandas as pd
    15	
    16	ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
    17	RAW = ROOT / "data" / "raw" / "broad_figshare"
    18	
    19	
    20	def load():
    21	    meta = pd.read_csv(RAW / "minipool_screen_metadata.csv")
    22	    eff = pd.read_csv(RAW / "minipool_chronos_gene_effects.csv").set_index("ScreenID")
    23	    lib = pd.read_csv(RAW / "minipool_library_composition.csv")
    24	    return meta, eff, lib
    25	
    26	
    27	def paired_deltas(meta: pd.DataFrame, eff: pd.DataFrame, contrast: str) -> pd.DataFrame:
    28	    """Per-gene within-model paired differences for one contrast type."""
    29	    rows = {}
    30	    if contrast == "medium":  # OPAC - RPMI/FBS, same model + format
    31	        groups = meta.groupby(["DepMap ID", "Growth Format"])
    32	        key, a, b = "Culture medium", "OPAC", "RPMI/FBS"
    33	    else:  # Dome - Plastic, same model + medium
    34	        groups = meta.groupby(["DepMap ID", "Culture medium"])
    35	        key, a, b = "Growth Format", "Dome", "Plastic"
    36	    for gkey, g in groups:
    37	        ga, gb = g[g[key] == a], g[g[key] == b]
    38	        if len(ga) == 1 and len(gb) == 1:
    39	            rows[f"{gkey[0]}|{gkey[1]}"] = eff.loc[ga.cell_line_name.iloc[0]] - eff.loc[gb.cell_line_name.iloc[0]]
    40	    return pd.DataFrame(rows)  # genes x pairs
    41	
    42	
    43	def main() -> dict:
    44	    meta, eff, lib = load()
    45	    test_genes = lib.loc[lib["All test genes"] == True, "Gene"].tolist()  # noqa: E712
    46	    out = {"n_screens": int(len(meta)), "n_models": int(meta["DepMap ID"].nunique()),
    47	           "models": sorted(meta["DepMap ID"].unique()), "n_test_genes_in_library": len(test_genes)}
    48	    frames = {}
    49	    for contrast in ("medium", "format"):
    50	        d = paired_deltas(meta, eff, contrast)
    51	        frames[contrast] = d
    52	        sub = d.loc[d.index.intersection(test_genes)]
    53	        out[contrast] = {
    54	            "n_pairs": int(d.shape[1]), "pair_keys": list(d.columns),
    55	            "median_abs_delta_test_genes": float(sub.abs().median().median()),
    56	            "frac_genes_sign_inconsistent": float(((sub > 0).any(axis=1) & (sub < 0).any(axis=1)).mean()),
    57	        }
    58	        d.to_csv(ROOT / "data" / f"paired_deltas_{contrast}.csv")
    59	    # Direction check vs published regression coefficients (C5 precursor).
    60	    coef = pd.read_csv(RAW / "minipool_regression_coefficients.csv")
    61	    out["published_coef_columns"] = list(coef.columns)
    62	    (ROOT / "data" / "identifiability_audit.json").write_text(json.dumps(out, indent=2))
    63	    print(json.dumps(out, indent=2))
    64	    return out
    65	
    66	
    67	if __name__ == "__main__":
    68	    main()

 succeeded in 0ms:
7. **Sol-high review → verify → fix → re-review to ≥ 90**, per-issue dispositions (`CONFIRMED+FIXED` / `ACCEPTED with claim downgrade` / `REJECTED with evidence`).
8. **`GENERATIONS.md` entry:** numbers for every finalist incl. failures, lessons, next mandate, stop-rule checks (leakage ⇒ dependent lines cancelled), and **multi-counter trial accounting** (cumulative, separate: unique configs, attempts, replications, hypothesis inspections, policy candidates, replay episodes, batteries — scientific vs engineering adaptation never merged into one total; no count substitutes for confirmatory procedure).

**0.5.2 Screening honesty.** A screen justifies exhaustive rejection **only** if passing it is a mathematically necessary condition for passing the battery (proof stated in SPEC, e.g. battery metric is a monotone refinement bounded by the screen metric). Otherwise verdicts read "no *tested finalist* passes" and screen-negative members stay listed as untested. Cheapest-falsification probes run as one fixed SPEC with no variants; failure closes only the tested specification, with family-level closure requiring the necessary-condition argument.

**0.5.3 Evaluator versioning (anchor is not certification).** Scores from different evaluator versions are **never pooled** in one comparison/replay pool. The frozen baseline "anchor" reproduction is a smoke check only. Any evaluator change requires: compatibility suite (rankings + validity decisions on a frozen probe set), explicit invalidation list for affected results, and — if substantive — the new-study/new-evidence procedure. Versions preserved side-by-side.

**0.5.4 Matched controls & post-hoc rule.** A new ingredient is credited only vs a same-construction control lacking it; unmatched construction changes are disclosed as non-decomposable. Post-hoc discoveries (e.g. control beats hypothesis) are flagged post-hoc, may be frozen for forward judging, never promotable on consumed data.

**0.5.5 Generation boundary (end-to-end).** Deterministic scores/frontier/episode views → sol-xhigh direction summary + ranked proposal (keep / recombine-with-rationale / repair / open / pause, all ID-cited) → policy code executes selection under budget → runs → extraction + predicate verification → sol-high ≥ 90 → ledger + trace-pool append.

### 0.6 Autonomy, unstick protocol, campaign limits & terminal determination
- **Default = continue autonomously** through failures/negative results, generation after generation, **within campaign allocations** (Lock 1: per-phase + total wall/nodes/model-calls, inside §0.2 ceilings). Legitimate stops: hard authorization gate (credential/spending/required file), allocation/ceiling exhaustion, explicit user stop, no-progress determination (below), terminal determination.
- **Unstick (sol-xhigh):** on stall (no obvious legal action, unexpected gate failure, all directions paused/dead/blocked, contradictory results, same failure twice) invoke sol-xhigh with the state packet (STATUS, episode views, frontier, last 3 generation entries, blocker + attempts) for ranked unblocks + cheapest discriminating experiment + draft next SPEC. Verify vs locked contract (sol cannot authorize contract/ceiling/final-access/standard changes); ledger consultation+disposition; **unstick calls are charged costs** (Phase 5). Max 3 consultation rounds per blocker, then smallest legal evidence-gathering action. Under a frozen comparison, unstick input is restricted to that arm's admissible history (§0.1 freeze).
- **No-progress / negative-EV rule (prevents indefinite retries, spec §14):** applies **before Lock 1 too**, against §0.2 pre-lock allocations: a pre-lock blocker (crosswalk, pairing, review gate) surviving its repeat-failure limit, or K consecutive generations (K default 3, finalized at Lock 1) with no frontier movement / new identifiable question / gate progress, triggers joint sol-xhigh + lead assessment; concurrence ⇒ admissible **non-complete terminal determination** (`INCONCLUSIVE`/`BLOCKED`) with unmet gates + smallest next step. Gate reviews exceeding the Phase-0 round cap escalate to the user with the residual issue list rather than looping.
- **Terminal determination (sol-xhigh judges, evidence rules):** `COMPLETE_*` requires sol-xhigh's written gate-by-gate (C1–C13) certification over the full evidence package, score ≥ 90, zero unresolved high/critical — **in addition to** the evidence, never instead of it; it cannot waive gates. A **pre-reserved terminal-review budget slice** (Lock 1) guarantees the call is affordable; if it still cannot run, the executor may mechanically checkpoint and report `BLOCKED`/`BUDGET_EXHAUSTED` honestly — but **positive completion always requires the sol-xhigh determination**. Non-complete determinations list exact unmet gates; consumed Layer-B/C evidence stays consumed (Phases 5–6).

## Phase 0 — Bootstrap (Gate G0)
Create `cancer-dependency-rsi/` (README, GOAL copy+hash, PROTOCOL stub, STATUS+status.json, `config/{resources,host}.json`, `src/`, `data/`, `ledger/`, `reviews/`, `artifacts/`, `generations/`, `directions/`, `trace_pool/`, `tests/`, `manuscript/`); project `.venv` via uv + lockfile; implement first: budget wrapper (§0.2 tests incl. concurrency/retries/interruption), append-only ledgers, artifact store, hashing, CLI skeleton (`bootstrap, audit-data, reproduce-baselines, run-pilot, run-cycle, evaluate-policy, lock-science, evaluate-external, reproduce, status, resume` — gate-locked commands fail loudly naming the gate). Record host fingerprint without secrets. **G0:** status/resume work; dummy over-budget job demonstrably killed (timeout+RSS); tests green; sol-high ≥ 90. *(Status 2026-10-28: scaffold + ledger/budget + 3 tests done; CLI + remaining tests pending.)*

## Phase 1 — Quarantine-first verification & identifiability audit (Gate G1 → C1–C3)
**1a. Metadata-only first.** Before importing any outcome-bearing file: create **provisional A/B/C partitions from metadata alone** (model/donor/lineage lists, no outcome values), write the **quarantine + exposure log** (every outcome-file access records who/what/when/which partition). Permitted pre-Lock-1 outcome contact: **Layer-A (development) sources only** (incl. the Broad minipool + genome-wide development data) and *published* QC summaries for B/C candidates. Sanger outcome matrices, once imported, sit quarantined as Layer-C candidates: schema/shape/control-gene inventory checks allowed via logged scripts; **no outcome-value inspection, ranking, or modeling** until Lock 2. Any accidental exposure is logged and downgrades affected claims.
**1b. Read primary sources in full** (S1 PMC13581591, S2 PMC13581617, S3 PMC13664187 + Zenodo 22472622, S5 arXiv:2609.14858 + repo): exact designs, selection rules, score definitions/directions, QC, published findings (= controls). *(Done at full-text level 2026-10-28; supplements/code deep-read continues.)*
**1c. Routes via official manifests only** — source URL, release/commit, date, license, bytes, SHA-256 per file into `data/source_manifest*.json`. Never invent URLs; never bypass challenges (DepMap portal verification page ⇒ use study-specific figshare releases, preferred anyway). **Leakage rule (spec §5, corrected):** if any correction/normalization/**target or model selection** used external outcomes, that evidence requires **development-only reconstruction or genuinely independent validation — disclosure never repairs it**; otherwise the independence gate stays **unmet**. Only **covariate-only** joint preprocessing may proceed as disclosed transductive preprocessing with limited claims. Audit Chronos pre-training + 26Q3 integration under this rule.
**1d. Provenance + crosswalk + counts:** model/donor/lineage/origin/lab/library/medium/format/replicate/release/outcome; Broad↔HCMI↔Sanger↔author IDs; uncertain donor = `uncertain`; count **independent models/donors** (genes/guides/wells/cultures ≠ patients); attrition registry. Verify seeds at row level. *(Minipool verified: 7 models, 18 screens, 5 medium + 7 format matched contrasts, 250 selected genes, 0 missing; ACH-003052 lineage gap + HCM-SANG↔ACH crosswalk open.)*
**1e. Assay audit (Layer-A + published summaries):** Chronos vs Sanger LFC/BAGEL — direction, range, controls, missingness, QC per source; absolute values never interchanged/subtracted. Confirm required pairings exist before modeling; check lab↔biology confounding.
**1f. Novelty audit v1:** dated search (queries/exclusions preserved) + claim ledger (nearest prior, established part, precise residual uncertainty, evidence needed, consequences). Rule out generic 2D→organoid transfer (S3) and published format/medium class findings (S1) as novelty.
**Gate G1:** named falsifiable question in a named subset + counted independent units + documented paired contrasts (or documented narrower question; broader component recorded unmet). No adaptive search before G1. Sol-high ≥ 90 on provenance + novelty package.

## Phase 2 — Vertical slice, baselines, Lock 1 (Gate G2 → C4–C5)
1. **Estimators (Layer-A only):** paired within-model contrasts in original units — both conditions, **all individual paired changes**, uncertainty, heterogeneity. **Joint uncertainty is mandatory with the correct independent unit:** the resampling unit is selected from the crosswalk — **donors** (resampling a donor carries *all* its nested models, cultures, contrasts, and every gene's paired vector together) whenever multiple models share a donor; models only when donor≡model is verified. Uncertain donor identity ⇒ conservative grouping (possibly-shared treated as shared) **plus** a sensitivity analysis under the split assumption; both reported. An explicit covariance model may substitute only with justification. Downstream transfer analyses refit per replicate; conclusions that flip are reported. Per-gene independent error bars alone are non-conforming. Effective sample sizes (donors *and* models) reported; few clusters ⇒ justify or downgrade intervals/tests. Tests include nested-donor fixtures.
2. **Baselines** (same rows/info; preprocessing/selection/tuning inside development folds only): lineage-only; genotype-only; state/expression-only; genotype+state; +context/assay; regularized-linear à la S3 (from Zenodo code where possible). Compare vs **strongest**; prespecified environmental ablation; matched controls for any new ingredient. Final-outcome reproductions deferred to post-Lock-2. **Fold construction matches the generalization claim (blocked evaluation, spec §9):** fold manifests are frozen artifacts; for unseen-donor prediction, **every conservatively-linked donor's models, cultures, and repeated observations sit in exactly one fold — including nested tuning folds** (donor-grouped CV, same conservative grouping as §Phase 2.1); lineage/target/pathway blocking added where the claim requires it. Row-level random folds are non-conforming for donor-generalization claims. (Within-model paired environmental contrasts are a separate design, not CV folds, and remain governed by §Phase 2.1.) Required fixture: distinct model IDs sharing one donor must never straddle folds — build fails otherwise.
3. **Evaluator v1** (separate module, narrow interface): effect sizes + calibrated predictive/uncertainty endpoints; **never** p-value size / discovery count / narrative confidence. Invalid rules: leakage, support violations, and **verified orientation/preprocessing errors** ⇒ `invalid`. A correctly measured **opposite-direction result is valid evidence** (potential refutation/heterogeneity), never invalidated for direction alone.
4. **Lock 1 — freeze contract** (`PROTOCOL.md` + `contract.json`, hashed with evaluator + splits + generator protocol). Mandatory fields: question + falsifiable alternatives; population/environments/targets/generalization; eligibility; data/ID versions; **final A/B/C partitions** (blocked, overlap-recorded, refining Phase-1a provisionals using metadata only); feature/transform rules; endpoints (primary/secondary, fixed direction, q-transform §0.4.3); **meaningful-dependency-strength requirement for every positive vulnerability claim** (predeclared threshold in each claimed environment, tested independently of the environmental-change criterion; stable non-dependency ≠ vulnerability); practical-effect/equivalence margins + justification (no margin ⇒ no "invariant" label); multiplicity (default Holm FWER 0.05, small family) + joint-uncertainty method; baselines; invalid rules; success/failure/inconclusive/exhaustion; **trajectory machinery** (BRANCHES/REFINE, status transition table, replay objective + ρ weights, support thresholds, episode sets); **policy-comparison design** (Phase 5: resource endpoint fully specified, # independent blocks, savings estimand, paired estimator, censoring-compatible CI, precision-feasibility result, cost-allocation rules); **campaign allocations + retry/no-progress limits + terminal-review reserve**; context/retrieval contract. Change ⇒ new study ID + new evidence.
5. **Tests:** leakage (donor overlap A/B/C fails build), score direction, grouping, synthetic-estimator behavior (code only), cost/reservation suite, resume idempotence, unsupported-replay, **hidden-outcome invariance** (§0.4.2), DAG acyclicity/version compat, final-outcome access guard + exposure log, evaluator-compatibility probe set.

SOURCE PMC13581617.xml

SECTION CRISPR–Cas9 screening Detailed protocols including process diagrams and example data for generating Cas9-expressing organoid cultures and CRISPR–Cas9 library transduction in organoid are published on protocols.io 93 , 94 . Stable Cas9-expressing organoids were generated using lentiCas9-Blast (Addgene 52962) with polybrene (8 μg ml −1 ). Following overnight incubation, medium was replaced with complete medium containing Y-27632 (2.5 μM), and blasticidin (Invivogen, ant-bl-1, 10 mg ml −1 ) selection was applied 120 h after transduction. Cas9 activity was measured using a fluorescent reporter assay (Addgene 67982 and 67981) 95 . Only organoid lines with activity of 75% or more were selected for single guide RNA (sgRNA) library transduction. Two genome-wide CRISPR–Cas9 sgRNA libraries were used. The Human CRISPR Library Yusa v.1.1 (ref.  3 ), containing 100,086 sgRNAs that target 18,009 genes (with 5–10 sgRNAs per gene) and 1,004 non-targeting sgRNAs; and the Minimal Genome-Wide Human CRISPR–Cas9 Library (MinLibCas9, Addgene 164896) 41 , which includes a selected sgRNA primarily drawn from Yusa v1.1 comprising 37,522 sgRNAs that target 18,761 genes (with 2 optimal sgRNAs per gene) and 200 non-targeting sgRNAs (Extended Data Fig.  8a ). Lentiviral volume required for multiplicity of infection of 0.3 was determined by titration, and transduction efficiency assessed by BFP flow cytometry. A total of 12.5 × 10 8  (Yusa v1.1) or 3.3 × 10 7  (MinLibCas9) cells were transduced in triplicate using the same batch of Cas9-transduced organoids. No significant batch effects were observed across independent batches of Cas9-expressing organoid lines (Extended Data Fig.  8b ). Cells were infected with the lentiviral-packaged whole-genome sgRNA library (for 100× coverage), in medium containing polybrene (8 μg ml −1 ), and Y-27632 (2.5 μM). Following overnight incubation, cells were plated in fresh medium in a 5% suspension. Transduction efficiency (target of 30%) was confirmed on day 6. Screens were maintained under puromycin selection (Invivogen, ant-pr-1, 10 mg ml −1 ) for a further 16 days (21 days in screen in total). A final selection efficiency of at least 60% was required for the screen to pass. At the end of the screen approximately 2.5 × 10 7  cells were collected, pelleted, and stored at −80 °C for downstream processing.

SECTION CRISPR screen data processing CRISPR screens performed using the Yusa v1.1 (ref.  3 ) and MinLibCas9 (ref.  41 ) libraries were harmonized by restricting analyses to shared sgRNAs, enabling integrated downstream processing. Quality control procedures, adapted from established cell line screening pipelines 3 , were applied to assess replicate concordance, sgRNA representation and classifier performance in distinguishing essential from non-essential genes. Low-quality replicates and organoids failing predefined quality control criteria were excluded prior to further analysis. Read counts were normalized and corrected for copy-number effects using CRISPRcleanR (v3.0.1) 96 , followed by batch correction across libraries 97 . Gene-level fitness effects were estimated using BAGEL (v2) 45  with curated reference gene sets, and LFC values were scaled relative to essential and non-essential controls to facilitate cross-organoid comparability. Full details of sgRNA selection, quality control thresholds, normalization, batch correction, statistical procedures and final selection of models are provided in the  Supplementary Information .

Main results of the analysis are presented in the  Supplementary Tables . In addition, genomic, transcriptomic and CRISPR processed data are available in the Cell Model Passports 40  and DepMap miner database.
