 Extended Data Fig. 8 Extended analyses of Wnt dependency and other cancer gene dependencies. a , Lack of constant association between Wnt-activating genetic alteration and Wnt regulator-dependency. All the CRISPR-screened organoids (n = 108) categorized into Wnt pathway gene alteration-positive (n = 30) or -negative (n = 78) groups based on mutations/CN alterations in  CTNNB1 ,  APC ,  AXIN2 , or  RNF43 . The positive group included 21  APC , 1  AXIN2 , 5  CTNNB1  hotspot, 5  RNF43  damaging mutations, and 1  CTNNB1  CN amplification, with some models harboring multiple alterations. Dependencies on the Wnt signaling pathway regulators ( MESD ,  WLS ,  TCF7L2 ,  FZD5  and  LGR4 ) were plotted for each of these two groups (left). The expression levels of the PDAC-classical/MUC program were also plotted. For the Wnt pathway gene alteration-positive (red box) group, the point colors indicate the type of alteration. Significance of the difference was assessed by two-tailed Mann-Whitney  U  test. The centre line and box denote median and 25th to 75th percentile data points, respectively, and whiskers extend up to 1.5 times the interquartile range from box edges. Harboring Wnt-activating alterations did not have constant effects on Wnt regulator dependencies: it reduced the dependencies on  MESD ,  WLS ,  FZD5  and  LGR4 , but enhanced  TCF7L2  dependency.  b , Mutation-independent association between PDAC-classical/MUC program expression and dependencies on Wnt regulators. The correlation between PDAC-classical/MUC program expression and dependency on the five regulators of Wnt signaling ( MESD ,  WLS ,  TCF7L2 ,  FZD5  and  LGR4 ) as well as their mean dependency scores in the organoid models were plotted, where Wnt-activating genetic alterations were indicated by the color code shown in  a . All the organoids with strong mean dependency scores on these Wnt regulators (mean Chronos score < −1;  n  = 21) exhibited enhanced PDAC-classical/MUC program expression (expression score > 2.8) regardless of Wnt-activating genetic alterations. Regression lines with 95% confidence intervals are also displayed.  c , Wnt dependency in the traditional cell lines and organoids. The mean dependency in the organoids ( n  = 108; y-axis) and lineage-matched traditional models (screen  n  = 485; x-axis) were plotted for all the members of the MSigDB ‘GOBP_WNT_SIGNALING_PATHWAY’ gene set. For the seven members that exhibited significant correlation with the PDAC-classical MP expression ( MESD ,  WLS ,  TCF7L2 ,  FZD5 ,  LGR4 ,  MARK2  and  SOX9 ; see Fig.  4d ), the NextGen organoids exhibited stronger dependency than lineage-matched traditional cell lines (mean[NextGen] > mean[traditional]). However, this held true for less than a half of the remaining members of this gene set (198/438 [45.2%]).  d , e , Differential dependencies on frequently mutated oncogenes and tumor suppressor genes (TSGs) between 2D models and 3D organoids. Dependency on frequently mutated (>1% mutation rate in the TCGA cohort 53 ) oncogenes and TSGs (40 oncogenes and 50 TSGs) were compared between the 3D organoids grown in serum-free conditions ( n  = 94) and lineage-matched 2D adherent models cultured directly on plastic plates with serum-containing media ( n  = 433). In  d , the mean dependency scores in 2D vs 3D models are represented in the x- and y-axes, respectively. Here and in  f , the color of the points indicates the type of the cancer genes (red: oncogenes, blue: TSGs) and the point sizes indicate the significance of the difference. Only 22 oncogenes and 20 TSGs that exhibited statistically significant ( q  < 0.05) difference of dependency were shown here, while in  c , the pathways displaying significant and non-significant ( q  ≥ 0.05) differences are distinguished by filled and open circles, respectively. We also compared the dependency on each of these cancer genes after removing the mutant (hotspot mutation for oncogenes; damaging mutations for TSGs) models for the respective gene, but it remained significant for all the 42 genes shown here. In  e , the distribution of dependency in individual models were plotted for two representative cancer genes ( CDK4  and  MDM2 ), where the lineage was indicated by the point color (see  g  for the legend). For  MDM2 , the dependency distribution in  TP53  WT models ( n  = 22 [3D organoids], 97 [2D models]) were also plotted (right). Here and in  g , the statistical significance was assessed by two-tailed Mann-Whitney  U  test.  f , g , A pathway-level summary of the differential dependencies on frequently mutated oncogenes and TSGs. Oncogenes and TSGs analyzed in  a  were classified into the 10 oncogenic signaling pathways in accordance with a previous publication 53 . For each of these 10 pathways, mean dependency scores on the oncogenes or TSGs that belong to the respective pathway were calculated and these mean scores were compared between 3D (y-axis;  n  = 94) and 2D (x-axis;  n  = 433) models ( f ). The pathway:cancer gene type (oncogene or TSG) pairs with two or more genes were plotted. The mean dependency scores of the individual models were also plotted for the TSGs of the p53 pathway ( g ).  h , Clustering of the gene sets that are enriched for differential dependencies between 2D models and 3D organoids. In the global comparison of gene dependencies, the nine gene sets with significant overrepresentation ( q  < 0.05 by one-sided Fisher’s exact test) of the 200 outlier genes (top 100 genes in either extreme) in the distribution of mean dependency differences (see Fig.  5a ) were clustered by the degree of overlap in their component genes. Note that this analysis was conducted not only on the selected cancer genes but on all the genes measured in the screen except for the common essential genes (as opposed to  d - g ). The number of member genes within each of these gene sets is: 200 (HALLMARK_OXIDATIVE_PHOSPHORYLATION), 200 (HALLMARK_MTORC1_SIGNALING), 200 (HALLMARK_G2M_CHECKPOINT), 139 (KEGG_SYSTEMIC_LUPUS_ERYTHEMATOSUS), 158 (HALLMARK_FATTY_ACID_METABOLISM), 200 (HALLMARK_E2F_TARGETS), 199 (KEGG_FOCAL_ADHESION), 213 (KEGG_REGULATION_OF_ACTIN_CYTOSKELETON), and 158 (HALLMARK_UV_RESPONSE_UP). Two pairs of gene sets exhibited a high-degree of overlap (overlap coefficient > 0.3) with each other and therefore we merged each of them into one gene set: • KEGG_FOCAL_ADHESION and KEGG_REGULATION_OF_ACTIN_CYTOSKELETON (merged and renamed as ‘Adhesion/Cytoskeleton’) • HALLMARK_G2M_CHECKPOINT and HALLMARK_E2F_TARGETS (‘Cell Cycle’) The remaining five gene sets, HALLMARK_FATTY_ACID_METABOLISM (‘Lipid Metabolism’), HALLMARK_MTORC1_SIGNALING (‘mTORC1’), HALLMARK_OXIDATIVE_PHOSPHORYLATION (‘OxPhos’), HALLMARK_UV_RESPONSE_UP (‘UV’), and KEGG_SYSTEMIC_LUPUS_ERYTHEMATOSUS (‘SLE’) were not merged with others. Also see Fig.  5a,b .

 Extended Data Fig. 9 CRISPR minipool screens in different growth formats and medium types. a , Composition of test genes in the minipool library ( n  = 250). The library for the minipool screen was designed primarily to evaluate the essentiality of genes that exhibit large differences in the systematic comparison of gene dependencies between 2D models and 3D organoids (see Fig.  5a,b ). These genes include those involved in the regulation of adhesion/cytoskeleton (further divided into “ECM Adhesion”, “Actin Regulation”, “Tight Junction”, “Adherens Junction” and “Integrin” classes) and the control of cholesterol homeostasis (as a part of the “Lipid Metabolism” class). They were categorized into nine core classes that are listed on the left based on the function of the encoded protein. Some of the test genes belong to multiple classes, while others do not belong to any of these classes but are still included in the library based on specific interests. The membership patterns of individual genes across the nine functional classes, along with the count of test genes exhibiting each specific pattern, are shown (top). The number of test genes that belong to each of the nine classes were also plotted (left).  b , Quality control (QC) test of individual minipool screens. Seven models were subjected to screening with the minipool library under different combinations of growth formats (adherent culture on plastic dishes [plastic] and Matrigel dome culture [dome]) and culture media (RPMI/FBS and OPAC serum-free media). 18 screens passed the criteria for QC, which were total readcounts ≥ 5 ×106 and null-normalized mean difference (NNMD) ≤ −1.5. These QC metrics for individual screens as well as the log fold change (LFC) correlation of replicate samples (where replicate samples were available) are presented.  c , Correlation between the genome-wide screen results and minipool screen results. The dependency scores from the genome-wide CRISPR screen (x-axis) and minipool screen (y-axis) were plotted for all the genes (nonessential [negative] controls, common essential [positive] controls and test genes) that were targeted by the minipool library for all the five models screened in minipool format under the same conditions as the genome-wide screens. This showed high concordance ( r  > 0.59) between the genome-wide screen result and minipool screen result for all these pairs.  d , e , Impact of the growth format and culture medium on the Chronos gene effect of representative genes measured by the minipool screen. All genes with significant differential dependency across conditions, in addition to  CDK4  and  GPX4 , are shown. In  d , the Chronos gene effects under the Matrigel dome culture (dome) and adherent culture on a plastic plate (plastic) were plotted for pairs of matched screens (same model type, same culture medium;  n  = 7). Results from cultures with the RPMI/FBS medium and OPAC medium are indicated by the blue and orange points, respectively. In  e , the Chronos gene effects under cultures with the RPMI/FBS medium (serum-containing) and OPAC medium (serum-free) were plotted for pairs of matched screens (same model type, same growth format;  n  = 5). Results from the dome culture and plastic culture are indicated by the blue and orange points, respectively. In both plots, the results from the same pair are indicated by the connecting line and the red horizontal line indicates the median gene effect score of the sample group. The statistical significance of the difference in the gene effects under dome vs plastic cultures as well as under RPMI/FBS vs OPAC media, assessed by a two-tailed paired  t -test, are also indicated. Genes whose dependency was significantly ( P  < 0.05) affected by the growth formats (dome vs plastic) or culture media (RPMI/FBS vs OPAC) are indicated by the asterisks. The dependencies on three integrin subunits ( ITGB1 ,  ITGAV  and  ITGA3 ) and  NCKAP1 , a regulator of cytoskeletal organization, were specifically influenced by the growth format but not by the culture medium. In contrast, the dependencies on three mediators of lipid metabolism ( SPTLC1 ,  SPTLC2  and  SQLE ) and  RICTOR , an oncogene in the PI3K/mTOR pathway, were affected by the culture medium but not by the growth format. The dependency on  PTK2 , a gene encoding FAK—a central mediator of adhesion-dependent signaling, was significantly altered by both growth format and culture medium. Here and in  f ,  g , the centre line and box denote median and 25th to 75th percentile data points, respectively, and whiskers extend up to 1.5 times the interquartile range from box edges.  f , g , The effects of growth formats and culture media on gene dependencies per gene class. Differential dependencies resulting from different growth formats (dome - plastic;  f ) and different culture media (OPAC - RPMI/FBS;  g ), both calculated as coordinates in a linear regression model (see  Methods ), were plotted for each of the nine classes (see  a ) of minipool test genes. None of these gene classes exhibited significant enrichment or depletion of dependencies (relative to the rest of the test genes) upon changes in growth format or culture medium, except for the effect of culture medium on ‘Actin Regulation’ gene dependencies, which were enhanced with the RPMI/FBS medium as compared to the OPAC medium ( q  = 0.0224 by a two-tailed Mann-Whitney  U  test; indicated by the asterisk).  n  = 75 (actin regulation), 30 (adherens junction), 53 (cell cycle), 89 (ECM adhesion), 22 (integrin), 20 (lipid metabolism), 20 (PI3K/mTOR signaling), 34 (tight junction) and 29 (WNT signaling).

 Extended Data Fig. 10 Growth formats and culture media respectively influence dependencies on integrins and cholesterol biosynthesis mediators. a , Specific effects of growth format and culture medium on the dependencies on integrin and regulators of cholesterol homeostasis. The KP3-enAsCas12a and HS766T-enAsCas12a cells were infected with lentivirus expressing cutting control sgRNAs (sgCh2 and sgAAVS), sgRNAs against pan-dependency genes (sgPOLR2D, sgSF3B1 and sgKIF11), or sgRNAs for indicated test genes. Following a puromycin selection to eliminate uninfected cells, these cells were propagated under different growth formats (monolayer culture on plastic dishes [plastic] vs Matrigel dome culture [dome]) and culture media (RPMI/FBS or OPAC medium) for seven days and viability scores relative to the average viability of cells transduced with cutting control sgRNAs were scored and plotted. Values = means ± SD ( n  = 3). The same experimental results were also presented in Fig.  5h , where the average viability of cells transduced with cutting control sgRNAs was set as 0, the average viability of cells with knockout (KO) of pan-dependency genes was set as -1 and the viability scores of cells that received sgRNAs against the test genes were plotted on this scale.  P -values were scored by two-tailed two-way ANOVA test (vs samples with cutting control sgRNAs [sgCh2 and sgAAVS]).  b , Colocalization of FAK with different integrin chains between plastic and dome growth formats. The KP3 cells were grown under plastic or dome conditions with the RPMI/FBS medium for five days and immunostained for either integrin β 1  and FAK or integrin α v  and FAK (see Fig.  5i ). Colocalization between integrin subunits and FAK was scored by calculating intensity correlation quotient, or Li’s ICQ 74 , on background-subtracted images. Shown are the aggregated results of two independent experiments.  P -values were scored by two-tailed Mann-Whitney  U  test.  n  = 20 (integrin β 1 /FAK staining, plastic), 19 (integrin β 1 /FAK staining, dome), 18 (integrin α V /FAK staining, plastic) and 21 (integrin α V /FAK staining, dome).  c , d , Flow cytometry analysis of cell-surface integrin β 1 /α v  expression under different growth formats. The KP3 cells, grown under plastic or dome conditions with the RPMI/FBS medium for 5 days, were stained for integrin β 1  and integrin α v  without permeabilization and analyzed by flow cytometry to assess the cell-surface expression levels of these integrin chains. The gating strategy for flow cytometry analysis is shown in  c . Thus, debris and dead cells were first excluded based on forward scatter-area (FSC-A) and side scatter-area (SSC-A) profiles. Subsequently, singlets were identified based on FSC-A and forward scatter-height (FSC-H) profiles. These singlets were then subjected to integrin expression analysis. The cell-surface expression level of integrin α v , but not that of integrin β 1 , was reduced to the negative control (isotype-matched IgG) level under the dome growth ( d ).  e , Differing roles of integrin β 1  and integrin α v  in controlling the localization of FAK under plastic vs dome culture conditions. KP3-enAsCas12a cells were infected with lentivirus expressing a cutting control sgRNA (sgCh2), or sgRNAs targeting  ITGB1  or  ITGAV . These cells were propagated under plastic or dome conditions with the RPMI/FBS medium for 4 days and immunostained for FAK (red). The nuclei of these cells were also visualized by DAPI staining (blue). In the control (sgCh2) cells, FAK accumulated to the cell periphery under both two culture conditions (dot-or oval-like patterns in plastic culture and uniform membrane staining in dome culture). The knockout (KO) of  ITGB1  prevented this accumulation specifically in the dome but not in plastic culture. In contrast, the KO of  ITGAV  prevented FAK localization to cell periphery only in the plastic but not dome culture. Representative images from three independent experiments are displayed. Bar = 20 μm.

To integrate data across models and libraries, we used Chronos 23 , which corrects for copy number (CN) effects, screen quality and library-specific batch effects (Fig.  1h,i ). Because all NextGen models used the Humagne library, library-specific batch effects were confounded with organoid model effects. We therefore pre-trained Chronos on all traditional and NextGen CNS models before applying the pretrained parameters to organoids. The integrated dataset—encompassing WGS, RNA-seq and CRISPR data and growth-format annotations—is available through the DepMap portal ( https://DepMap.org/portal ) 24 . 

To broadly compare gene essentialities, we calculated the difference in mean dependency scores between 3D and 2D models for every gene ( n  = 16,691; excluding common essential genes), and identified gene sets (Hallmark and KEGG gene sets from MSigDB;  q  < 0.05) that were significantly overrepresented at the extremes of this difference (Fig.  5a,b ). Clustering these gene sets by component overlap (>0.3; Extended Data Fig.  8h ) merged two sets (adhesion and cytoskeleton (adhesion–cytoskeleton), and cell cycle), whereas five others (lipid metabolism, mTORC1, oxidative phosphorylation, UV response and systemic lupus erythematosus) remained separate. Fig. 5 Comparing 2D and 3D dependencies to disentangle the effects of culture conditions. a , b , Gene sets enriched for differential dependencies between 3D and 2D models ( n  = 94 (3D) and 433 (2D)). Odds ratios for the 9 significant gene sets ( q  < 0.05) overrepresented in the 200 outlier genes were plotted, clustered and coloured by gene overlap (>0.3;  a ). Mean dependency differences and their significance for individual genes (two-tailed Mann–Whitney  U- test; gene  n  = 3,521), coloured by the gene set clusters from  a  were also plotted ( b ).  c – e , Minipool screens were performed to elucidate the differing effects of growth format and culture medium. A minipool screen (250 test genes) was designed ( c ) to separate the effects of growth format (plastic versus dome) and culture medium (RPMI–FBS versus OPAC) on gene essentiality. The screens identified genes for which dependencies are primarily altered by growth format ( d ) or culture medium ( e ), with red dashed lines indicating significance threshold ( q  = 0.05, two-sided linear regression  t -test) and the point colours indicating gene classes in the minipool library (bottom). Screen  n  = 11 (dome), 7 (plastic), 9 (OPAC), 9 (RPMI–FBS).  f , g , Comparisons across growth formats and culture media.  f , Different gene classes represented in the minipool library are highlighted. Integrin and actin regulation classes exhibited significant variance across growth formats.  g , By contrast, lipid metabolism class were affected by culture medium ( q  < 0.05, two-tailed  F -test; gene  n  = 200).  h , Differential effects on integrin and cholesterol homeostasis dependencies assessed by viability after test gene knockout. Values are the mean ± s.d. ( n  = 3).  P  values calculated using two-tailed two-way ANOVA. R–F, RPMI–FBS.  i , Colocalization of integrin chains (green) with FAK (red) in KP3 cells under plastic or dome growth conditions (both in RPMI–FBS). Nuclei (blue) and actin (white) are also shown. Representative images from three independent experiments are displayed. Scale bar, 20 μm. Source data 

a , b , Gene sets enriched for differential dependencies between 3D and 2D models ( n  = 94 (3D) and 433 (2D)). Mean dependency differences and their significance for individual genes (two-tailed Mann–Whitney  U- test; gene  n  = 3,521), coloured by the gene set clusters from  a  were also plotted ( b ).  c – e , Minipool screens were performed to elucidate the differing effects of growth format and culture medium. A minipool screen (250 test genes) was designed ( c ) to separate the effects of growth format (plastic versus dome) and culture medium (RPMI–FBS versus OPAC) on gene essentiality. The screens identified genes for which dependencies are primarily altered by growth format ( d ) or culture medium ( e ), with red dashed lines indicating significance threshold ( q  = 0.05, two-sided linear regression  t -test) and the point colours indicating gene classes in the minipool library (bottom). Screen  n  = 11 (dome), 7 (plastic), 9 (OPAC), 9 (RPMI–FBS).  f , g , Comparisons across growth formats and culture media.  f , Different gene classes represented in the minipool library are highlighted. Integrin and actin regulation classes exhibited significant variance across growth formats.  g , By contrast, lipid metabolism class were affected by culture medium ( q  < 0.05, two-tailed  F -test; gene  n  = 200).  h , Differential effects on integrin and cholesterol homeostasis dependencies assessed by viability after test gene knockout. Values are the mean ± s.d. ( n  = 3).  P  values calculated using two-tailed two-way ANOVA. R–F, RPMI–FBS.  i , Colocalization of integrin chains (green) with FAK (red) in KP3 cells under plastic or dome growth conditions (both in RPMI–FBS). Nuclei (blue) and actin (white) are also shown. Representative images from three independent experiments are displayed. Scale bar, 20 μm. 

Accordingly, we generated a focused enAsCas12a sgRNA minipool library targeting 250 genes from 9 core functional classes (5 related to adhesion–cytoskeleton, 1 for lipid metabolism and an additional 3 from the 3D versus 2D dependency comparison; Fig.  5c , Extended Data Fig.  9a  and Supplementary Table  3 ). Viability screens using this library were conducted across seven models, testing combinations of growth format (2D plastic or 3D Matrigel dome) and medium type (serum-containing RPMI–FBS or serum-free OPAC) for a total of 18 screens (Extended Data Fig.  9b ). 

After applying Chronos to the minipool screen data (which correlated well with genome-wide data,  r  > 0.59; Extended Data Fig.  9c ), we used linear regression to distinguish growth-format and culture-medium effects. This analysis identified seven genes for which dependencies were altered by growth formats ( q  < 0.05), including three integrin subunits ( ITGB1 ,  ITGAV  and  ITGA3 ) and two actin regulators ( NCKAP1  and  PTK2 ; Fig.  5d  and Extended Data Fig.  9d ). Eight genes also exhibited dependency changes across different media, three of which regulate lipid metabolism ( SPTLC1 ,  SPTLC2  and  SQLE ; Fig.  5e  and Extended Data Fig.  9e ). 

A global analysis of the minipool screen results across the nine functional gene classes did not reveal collective enhancement or impairment of dependencies between different growth formats and media, except for a minor enhancement (Δdependency = 0.09) in the actin regulation class under RPMI–FBS conditions compared with OPAC (Extended Data Fig.  9f,g ). To capture opposing effects in gene classes (for example,  ITGB1  dependency increases whereas  ITGAV  decreases in dome culture), we analysed the variance in dependency score differences between conditions (dome versus plastic and OPAC versus RPMI–FBS). Growth format had significantly larger effects on integrin ( q  = 7.3 × 10 −5 ) and actin regulation genes ( q  = 7.2 × 10 −14 ), whereas culture medium significantly affected lipid metabolism genes ( q  = 1.5 × 10 −4 ; Fig.  5f,g ). These data demonstrate functional class-specific effects of growth format and medium. 

For the minipool screens, to evaluate the effects of growth formats and culture media on gene essentiality, we decided to select test genes from nine different functional classes. Most of these gene classes are related to the regulation of adhesion–cytoskeleton, lipid metabolism or other processes highlighted in the systematic comparison of genome-wide CRISPR screen data between 2D models and 3D organoids (Fig.  5a,b ). The following gene classes were included: integrin (genes encoding an integrin α or β subunit); ECM adhesion (adhesome,  https://adhesome.org/ ); actin regulation (MSigDB gene set and KEGG regulation of actin cytoskeleton); adherens junction (MSigDB gene set and KEGG adherens junction); tight junction (MSigDB gene set and KEGG tight junction); lipid metabolism (a union of two MSigDB gene sets, Hallmark cholesterol homeostasis and Reactome metabolism of lipids); WNT signalling (MSigDB gene set and GOBP WNT signalling pathway); PI3K–mTOR signalling (MSigDB gene set and Hallmark PI3K–mTOR signalling); cell cycle (MSigDB gene set and GOBP cell cycle); and other genes of particular interest (manual curation). To select 250 test genes from these 9 core functional gene classes, we used the dependency profiles of the gene between 2D models and 3D organoids to prioritize genes that exhibited selective enrichment of dependency in either of these model types. Many of these test genes belong to multiple different classes, and the patterns of membership of individual genes across the nine classes and the number of test genes that exhibit the specific pattern are presented in Extended Data Fig.  9a . We then added 650 non-dependency genes and 100 pan-dependency genes, genes that are universally essential across 2D models and 3D organoids, to compile a list of 1,000 genes (Supplementary Table  3 ). We subsequently designed 4 enAsCas12a sgRNA sequences for each of these 1,000 genes using the CRISPick web tool ( https://portals.broadinstitute.org/gppx/crispick/public ), which were assembled into 2 constructs (2 sgRNA sequences for the same gene per construct; 2 constructs per gene) of the pRDA_052 backbone (Addgene, 136474). The resulting library with 2,000 constructs was used for minipool screening. 

Minipool screens were evaluated for sequencing quality by having a total read count of at least 500,000 reads across replicates. Gene scores and NNMDs were computed in the same manner as the genome-wide screens. Replicate correlation was computed as the Pearson’s correlation coefficient over log fold changes in all non-control genes. Screens with NNMDs greater than –1.5 and replicate correlations less than 0.2 were excluded from downstream analyses. Screens with a single replicate were retained if their other metrics passed these quality control thresholds. Chronos scores were calculated as previously described 23 , with adjusted hyperparameters (smoothing regularization parameter = 0.3, kernel width = 5) for the smaller library size. Subsequently, we modelled the Chronos score  Y g , k  for each gene  g  and screen  k  with the generalized linear regression model: \documentclass[12pt]{minimal}
                                                  \usepackage{amsmath}
                                                  \usepackage{wasysym}
                                                  \usepackage{amsfonts}
                                                  \usepackage{amssymb}
                                                  \usepackage{amsbsy}
                                                  \usepackage{mathrsfs}
                                                  \usepackage{upgreek}
                                                  \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt}
                                                  \begin{document}$${Y}_{g,k}={\beta }_{0}+{\beta }_{d}{I}_{d}+{\beta }_{s}{I}_{s}+{\beta }_{{ds}}{I}_{d}{I}_{s}+\sum _{m\in M}{\beta }_{m}{I}_{m}$$\end{document} Y g , k = β 0 + β d I d + β s I s + β ds I d I s + ∑ m ∈ M β m I m 

To assess the effects of growth format (or culture medium) on the core functional classes of gene targets included in our minipool library, we calculated the variance in  β d  (or  β s ) across members of the same class and compared it to the variance in  β d  (or  β s ) across all non-control genes outside of class. For each gene set, we tested whether the in-class variance was significantly greater than the out-of-class variance using one-tailed  F -tests. 

For Fig.  5d,e , in the minipool screen analysis, the effects of growth format (dome – plastic) and culture medium (OPAC – RPMI–FBS) on gene dependency (Chronos score) were determined as coefficients in a linear regression model (see  Methods , ‘Minipool screen analysis’), which was also used for calculating the  P  values against the null hypothesis that these variables have no effect on gene dependency (two-sided linear regression  t -test). The number of screens for each condition is as follows: dome format,  n  = 11; plastic format,  n  = 7; OPAC medium,  n  = 9; RPMI–FBS medium,  n  = 9. 

For Fig.  5g , the variances of dependency between different growth formats and different culture media were scored for all nine of the core functional classes of minipool target genes. The variances were compared between in-class genes and out-of-class genes (that is, rest of the 250 test genes in the minipool library) for each of the 9 core functional classes using one-tailed  F -tests. The functional class is considered to be significantly affected by the growth format or culture medium if the corresponding  q  value was less than 0.05. The numbers of genes in each of the 9 core functional classes are as follows: actin regulation,  n  = 75; adherens junction,  n  = 30; cell cycle,  n  = 53; ECM adhesion,  n  = 89; integrin,  n  = 22; lipid metabolism,  n  = 20; PI3K–mTOR signalling,  n  = 20; tight junction,  n  = 34; WNT signalling,  n  = 29. 

For Extended Data Fig.  9c , Pearson’s correlation of the gene dependency scores between the genome-wide screen and minipool screen was calculated across all the genes tested in the minipool screens ( n  = 1,000). Five pairs of genome-wide and minipool screens (KP3 plastic versus RPMI–FBS; HS766T plastic versus RPMI–FBS; UWB1289 plastic versus RPMI–FBS; PANFR0185T2 dome versus OPAC; and HCMBROD115C16 dome versus OPAC), for which these two types of screens were conducted under the same culture conditions (growth format and culture medium), were evaluated. 

For Extended Data Fig.  9d,e , for the seven pairs of minipool screens that were conducted with the same model, same medium but different growth format (dome versus plastic), the Chronos dependency scores of all the test genes in the minipool library ( n  = 250) were compared using two-tailed paired  t -tests (d). Similarly, for the five pairs of minipool screens that were conducted with the same model, same format but different culture medium (OPAC versus RPMI–FBS), the Chronos dependency scores of all the test genes in the minipool library were also compared using two-tailed paired  t -tests (e). All genes with significant differential dependencies in either of these comparisons as well as  CDK4  and  GPX4  are shown in the plots. 

For Extended Data Fig.  9f,g , effects of growth formats (f) and culture media (g) on gene dependencies (calculated by a linear regression model (see the section ‘Minipool screen analysis’ of the  Methods )) were compared between in-class genes and out-of-class genes (that is, the rest of the 250 test genes in the minipool library) for each of the 9 core functional classes of the minipool library. Significance of the difference was assessed by two-tailed Mann–Whitney  U- test. The numbers of genes in each of the 9 core functional classes are as follows: actin regulation,  n  = 75; adherens junction,  n  = 30; cell cycle,  n  = 53; ECM adhesion,  n  = 89; integrin,  n  = 22; lipid metabolism,  n  = 20; PI3K–mTOR signalling,  n  = 20; tight junction,  n  = 34; and WNT signalling,  n  = 29.

 Peer Review File 
 

Minipool screen library gene composition and analysis results. 


 Extended Data Fig. 9 CRISPR minipool screens in different growth formats and medium types. a , Composition of test genes in the minipool library ( n  = 250). The library for the minipool screen was designed primarily to evaluate the essentiality of genes that exhibit large differences in the systematic comparison of gene dependencies between 2D models and 3D organoids (see Fig.  5a,b ). These genes include those involved in the regulation of adhesion/cytoskeleton (further divided into “ECM Adhesion”, “Actin Regulation”, “Tight Junction”, “Adherens Junction” and “Integrin” classes) and the control of cholesterol homeostasis (as a part of the “Lipid Metabolism” class). They were categorized into nine core classes that are listed on the left based on the function of the encoded protein. Some of the test genes belong to multiple classes, while others do not belong to any of these classes but are still included in the library based on specific interests. The membership patterns of individual genes across the nine functional classes, along with the count of test genes exhibiting each specific pattern, are shown (top). The number of test genes that belong to each of the nine classes were also plotted (left).  b , Quality control (QC) test of individual minipool screens. Seven models were subjected to screening with the minipool library under different combinations of growth formats (adherent culture on plastic dishes [plastic] and Matrigel dome culture [dome]) and culture media (RPMI/FBS and OPAC serum-free media). 18 screens passed the criteria for QC, which were total readcounts ≥ 5 ×106 and null-normalized mean difference (NNMD) ≤ −1.5. These QC metrics for individual screens as well as the log fold change (LFC) correlation of replicate samples (where replicate samples were available) are presented.  c , Correlation between the genome-wide screen results and minipool screen results. The dependency scores from the genome-wide CRISPR screen (x-axis) and minipool screen (y-axis) were plotted for all the genes (nonessential [negative] controls, common essential [positive] controls and test genes) that were targeted by the minipool library for all the five models screened in minipool format under the same conditions as the genome-wide screens. This showed high concordance ( r  > 0.59) between the genome-wide screen result and minipool screen result for all these pairs.  d , e , Impact of the growth format and culture medium on the Chronos gene effect of representative genes measured by the minipool screen. All genes with significant differential dependency across conditions, in addition to  CDK4  and  GPX4 , are shown. In  d , the Chronos gene effects under the Matrigel dome culture (dome) and adherent culture on a plastic plate (plastic) were plotted for pairs of matched screens (same model type, same culture medium;  n  = 7). Results from cultures with the RPMI/FBS medium and OPAC medium are indicated by the blue and orange points, respectively. In  e , the Chronos gene effects under cultures with the RPMI/FBS medium (serum-containing) and OPAC medium (serum-free) were plotted for pairs of matched screens (same model type, same growth format;  n  = 5). Results from the dome culture and plastic culture are indicated by the blue and orange points, respectively. In both plots, the results from the same pair are indicated by the connecting line and the red horizontal line indicates the median gene effect score of the sample group. The statistical significance of the difference in the gene effects under dome vs plastic cultures as well as under RPMI/FBS vs OPAC media, assessed by a two-tailed paired  t -test, are also indicated. Genes whose dependency was significantly ( P  < 0.05) affected by the growth formats (dome vs plastic) or culture media (RPMI/FBS vs OPAC) are indicated by the asterisks. The dependencies on three integrin subunits ( ITGB1 ,  ITGAV  and  ITGA3 ) and  NCKAP1 , a regulator of cytoskeletal organization, were specifically influenced by the growth format but not by the culture medium. In contrast, the dependencies on three mediators of lipid metabolism ( SPTLC1 ,  SPTLC2  and  SQLE ) and  RICTOR , an oncogene in the PI3K/mTOR pathway, were affected by the culture medium but not by the growth format. The dependency on  PTK2 , a gene encoding FAK—a central mediator of adhesion-dependent signaling, was significantly altered by both growth format and culture medium. Here and in  f ,  g , the centre line and box denote median and 25th to 75th percentile data points, respectively, and whiskers extend up to 1.5 times the interquartile range from box edges.  f , g , The effects of growth formats and culture media on gene dependencies per gene class. Differential dependencies resulting from different growth formats (dome - plastic;  f ) and different culture media (OPAC - RPMI/FBS;  g ), both calculated as coordinates in a linear regression model (see  Methods ), were plotted for each of the nine classes (see  a ) of minipool test genes. None of these gene classes exhibited significant enrichment or depletion of dependencies (relative to the rest of the test genes) upon changes in growth format or culture medium, except for the effect of culture medium on ‘Actin Regulation’ gene dependencies, which were enhanced with the RPMI/FBS medium as compared to the OPAC medium ( q  = 0.0224 by a two-tailed Mann-Whitney  U  test; indicated by the asterisk).  n  = 75 (actin regulation), 30 (adherens junction), 53 (cell cycle), 89 (ECM adhesion), 22 (integrin), 20 (lipid metabolism), 20 (PI3K/mTOR signaling), 34 (tight junction) and 29 (WNT signaling).

F.V. conceived and initiated the study and oversaw the study. J.V.N. and L.B. optimized the protocol for genome-wide CRISPR screening of NextGen models and conducted screening and molecular profiling together with K.W., H.I., T.B., A.A., M.W., M.E., E.L., B.V., N.S., M.H., N.L., S.J.W., H. Liang, K.Y., X.Z., L.G., H.H. and S.D., which was supervised by D.E.R. and F.V. T.S. designed the analytical strategy and conducted data analysis, interpretation and figure preparation together with S.M., D.E. and Y.Y. K.W., H.I. and S.S. curated metadata and provided project-management support. C.E.M., M.T., K.P., H. Li, P.S.P., S.H. and Z.H.K. performed the CDK4/6 inhibitor experiments, supervised by P.K.S. J.V.N., M.E., N.L., H.S. and T.S. performed the minipool screens and validation experiments. L.B. and T.S. conducted the SCD inhibitor validation experiment. S.M., D.E., I.B., X.Z., L.G., P.M., D.G., J.D., C.K.W., J.A.G., C.D.C. and J.M.D. developed analytical and software tools. R.M.G., C.H., J.R.W., G.G., S.P., Y.B. and R.J. derived GBM models under the supervision of R.B. and K.L.L. D.D.B., S.B., P.S.W., S.R., E.C., D.L.S., A.J.B., Y.C., S.J.H., S.O., A.V.L., H.B., J.S.B., Y.-Y.T., W.C.H. and A.J.A. contributed to organoid derivation. The initial draft of the manuscript was prepared by T.S. with input from J.V.N., S.M., L.B., D.E. and F.V., which was shaped and finalized by S.M., T.S. and F.V. The funding for the study was obtained by J.S.B., W.C.H., A.J.A., K.L.L. and F.V.
